), it remains to prove the stochastic domination in (
5.18
).

Showing that the auxiliary graph dominates ERRG.

We now prove (
5.18
) case-by-case for the three regimes, for
\zeta
being either
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}
,
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}
, or
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}
. We reveal the subgraph pre-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
in (
5.10
), i.e., all induced subgraphs inside boxes
({\mathcal{Q}}_{j})_{j\in[m_{n}]}
, and assume that the realization satisfies
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}
. The first
\lceil(1-\varepsilon_{1})m_{n}\rceil
boxes are good and these labels form the vertex set
{\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}
. Each label corresponds to a component
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
. Edges between these components
({\mathcal{C}}_{j})_{j\in{\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}}
have not been revealed yet, and conditional on pre-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
, they are present independently of each other. Therefore, it is sufficient to show that for all
j,\ell\in{\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}
,

(5.19)

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{C}}_{j}\sim_{{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}\mid\text{pre-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\big)\geq\frac{\lambda}{|{\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}|}.

We will prove this by suitably choosing the constant
M
in (
5.7
), case-by-case for the possible values of
\zeta=\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}\in\{\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}\}
.

1. High-low edges,
\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}=\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}>0
.

We assume that
\alpha<\infty
, since
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}=(\tau-1)/\alpha-(\tau-2)<0
otherwise.
If a box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
is good, see (
5.11
), the large component
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
with size at least
\rho k_{n}
contains at least one vertex
v_{j}^{\star}
of mark at least
\varepsilon_{2}k_{n}^{1/(\tau-1)}
. If there are more such vertices, choose one arbitrarily.
We estimate the probability that
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}
are connected from below by the probability that either
v_{j}^{\star}
connects to any vertex in
{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}
or
v_{\ell}^{\star}
connects to any vertex in
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
. We bound marks in the other component from below by
1
. Any two vertices in
\Lambda_{n}
are within distance
\sqrt{d}n^{1/d}
. Since vertex weights are revealed in pre-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
, edges are present independently. So, we obtain from the connectivity probability
\mathrm{p}
in (
4.6
):

(5.20)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{C}}_{j}\nsim_{{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}\mid\text{pre-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\{v_{j}^{\star}\nsim_{{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}\}\cap\{v_{\ell}^{\star}\nsim_{{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}{\mathcal{C}}_{j}\}\mid\text{pre-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\big)

\displaystyle=\prod_{u\in{\mathcal{C}}_{j}}(1-\mathrm{p}(u,v_{\ell}^{\star}))\cdot\prod_{\tilde{u}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}}(1-\mathrm{p}(\tilde{u},v_{j}^{\star}))

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1-p\Big(1\wedge\frac{\beta\varepsilon_{2}k_{n}^{1/(\tau-1)}}{d^{d/2}n}\Big)^{\alpha}\Big)^{2\rho k_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(-2\rho k_{n}p(1\wedge\beta^{\alpha}d^{-\alpha d/2}\varepsilon_{2}^{\alpha}k_{n}^{\alpha/(\tau-1)}n^{-\alpha}\big)\Big).

There are now two cases. If the minimum is attained at
1
in the last row, then the right-hand side tends to
0
since
k_{n}=Mn^{1-\zeta}\to\infty
since
\zeta<1
. This means that (
5.19
) holds.
If the minimum is not attained at
1
, some further calculations are required.
We set
M
as the solution of the equation

(5.21)

\rho p\beta^{\alpha}d^{-\alpha d/2}\varepsilon_{2}^{\alpha}=M^{-\alpha/(\tau-1)}\lambda/(1-\varepsilon_{1}).

Then the last row in (
5.20
) equals

\exp\big(-2\rho p\beta^{\alpha}d^{-\alpha d/2}\varepsilon_{2}^{\alpha}k_{n}^{1+\alpha/(\tau-1)}n^{-\alpha}\big)=\exp\big(-\tfrac{2\lambda}{1-\varepsilon_{1}}k_{n}(k_{n}/M)^{\alpha/(\tau-1)}n^{-\alpha}\big).

We now use that
k_{n}=n/m_{n}
for the first appearance of
k_{n}
and that
k_{n}/M=n^{1-\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}}
in the second appearance, see (
5.8
). The exponent there is
1-\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}=(1-1/\alpha)(\tau-1)
, so rewriting the right-hand side yields that the exponents of
n
cancel each other. Using also
|{\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}|=\lceil(1-\varepsilon_{1})m_{n}\rceil
,

\exp\big(-\tfrac{2\lambda}{1-\varepsilon_{1}}(n/m_{n})n^{(1-\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}})\alpha/(\tau-1)}n^{-\alpha}\big)=\exp\Big(-\frac{2\lambda}{(1-\varepsilon_{1})m_{n}}\Big)<1-\frac{\lambda}{|{\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}|}

for all
n
sufficiently large. This proves the desired bound (
5.19
) in the high-low regime.

2. High-high edges,
\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}=\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}>0
, and
\alpha<\infty
.

As in the high-low regime, we reveal pre-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
, and choose in each good box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
a vertex
v_{j}^{\star}
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
with mark at least
\varepsilon_{2}k_{n}^{1/(\tau-1)}
, see (
5.11
). We directly estimate the probability that
v_{j}^{\star}
is connected to
v_{\ell}^{\star}
for
j,\ell\in{\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}
:

(5.22)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{C}}_{j}\sim_{{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}\mid\text{pre-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(v_{j}^{\star}\sim_{{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}v_{\ell}^{\star}\mid\text{pre-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n})

\displaystyle\geq p\Big(1\wedge\frac{\beta\varepsilon_{2}^{\sigma+1}k_{n}^{(\sigma+1)/(\tau-1)}}{d^{d/2}n}\Big)^{\alpha}.

If the minimum is attained at
1
on the right-hand side, then (
5.19
) follows, so we assume it is attained at the second term. We use that
k_{n}=n/m_{n}
by (
5.8
). The right-hand side equals

\displaystyle p\beta^{\alpha}d^{-\alpha d/2}\varepsilon_{2}^{(\sigma+1)\alpha}n^{\alpha(\sigma+1)/(\tau-1)-\alpha}m_{n}^{-\alpha(\sigma+1)/(\tau-1)+1}\frac{1}{m_{n}},

where we multiplied by a factor
m_{n}/m_{n}
. We use now that
m_{n}=n^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}/M
by (
5.7
) in the first occurrence of
m_{n}
above to compute the power of
n
. The above expression equals

p\beta^{\alpha}d^{-\alpha d/2}\varepsilon_{2}^{(\sigma+1)\alpha}M^{\alpha(\sigma+1)/(\tau-1)-1}n^{(1-\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}})\alpha(\sigma+1)/(\tau-1)-\alpha+\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}\cdot\frac{1}{m_{n}}.

It is an elementary computation to check that the power of
n
is
0
using that

\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}=(\sigma+1-(\tau-1))/(\sigma+1-(\tau-1)/\alpha)

in (
2.14
).
Further,
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}
is only positive when
\sigma>\tau-2
. This latter inequality implies that
(\sigma+1)/(\tau-1)>1
and so the exponent of
M
is strictly positive for all
\alpha>1
.
So, if we choose
M
large enough then the constant multiplying
1/m_{n}
in the above formula becomes arbitrarily large. So, returning to (
5.22
),

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{C}}_{j}\sim_{{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}\mid\text{pre-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\big)\geq\frac{1}{m_{n}}\cdot p\beta^{\alpha}d^{-\alpha d/2}\varepsilon_{2}^{(\sigma+1)\alpha}M^{\alpha(\sigma+1)/(\tau-1)-1}\geq\frac{\lambda}{|{\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}|}

holds for sufficiently large
M
, since
|{\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}|=\lceil(1-\varepsilon_{1})m_{n}\rceil
. This finishes the proof of (
5.18
) for the high-high regime with
\alpha<\infty
.

3. High-high edges,
\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}=\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}=(\sigma+2-\tau)/(\sigma+1)>0
, and
\alpha=\infty
.
This case is almost the same as the
\alpha<\infty
case. The only difference is that in (
5.22
) the minimum on the right-hand side
must
be attained at
1
, and then the connection probability is
p
, otherwise it is
0
.
So we check whether

\beta\varepsilon_{2}^{\sigma+1}k_{n}^{(1+\sigma)/(\tau-1)}/(d^{d/2}n)\geq 1

holds.
We use
k_{n}=Mn^{1-\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}
in (
5.8
)
and
1-\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}=\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}(\tau-1)=(\tau-1)/(\sigma+1)
when
\alpha=\infty
. Thus, the powers of
n
cancel each other again. Since
\tau<2+\sigma
, we can choose
M
large enough so that

\beta\varepsilon_{2}^{\sigma+1}M^{(1+\sigma)/(\tau-1)}/d^{d/2}\geq 1.

The bound (
5.19
) also follows in this regime.

4. Low-low edges,
\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}=\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}>0
.

We may assume that
\alpha<\infty
, since otherwise
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}=2-\alpha<0
. By the definition of a good box in (
5.11
),

|{\mathcal{C}}_{j}|\geq\rho k_{n}
and each vertex has mark at least
1
. So we compute using the connection probability
\mathrm{p}
in (
4.6
) for
j,\ell\in{\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}
:

(5.23)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{C}}_{j}\nsim_{{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}\mid\text{pre-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\big(1-p\beta^{\alpha}d^{-\alpha d/2}n^{-\alpha}\big)^{|{\mathcal{C}}_{j}|\cdot|{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}|}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-p\beta^{\alpha}d^{-\alpha d/2}\rho^{2}k_{n}^{2}n^{-\alpha}\big).

Since
k_{n}=Mn^{1-\zeta}=Mn^{\alpha-1}
in (
5.8
),
k_{n}^{2}n^{-\alpha}=M^{2}n^{\alpha-2}
. With
m_{n}=n^{2-\alpha}/M
, we may write
k_{n}^{2}n^{-\alpha}=M/m_{n}
on the right-hand side above. We choose
M
as the solution of the equation

p\beta^{\alpha}d^{-\alpha d/2}\rho^{2}M=2\lambda/(1-\varepsilon_{1}).

Now we obtain for
n
large that, using
|{\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}|=\lceil(1-\varepsilon_{1})m_{n}\rceil
,

(5.24)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{C}}_{j}\nsim_{{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}\mid\text{pre-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\big)

\displaystyle=\exp\big(-p\beta^{\alpha}d^{-\alpha d/2}\rho^{2}M\tfrac{1}{m_{n}}\big)\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{2\lambda}{|{\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}|}\bigg)<1-\frac{\lambda}{|{\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}|},

showing (
5.19
).
The same calculation works for long-range percolation on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
.

Conclusion for proving (
5.5
).
We conclude that the bound (
5.19
) holds whenever
\zeta
equals either
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}
for a suitable choice of
M
in (
5.7
). This proves the stochastic domination stated in (
5.18
) and finishes the proof of (
5.5
) in
Proposition

5.1
.
∎

5.2.
When relying on constant-length edges

We proceed to proving
Proposition

5.1
(short). Here, instead of using long edges to connect boxes and a domination towards an Erdős-Rényi random graph, we use “relatively short” edges and stochastic domination towards nearest neighbor site-bond percolation on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
. In that model,
each vertex
x\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
is independently
active
with probability
q
, and each edge between active neighboring vertices is
open
with probability
r
, independently again. We denote the resulting graph formed by open edges between active vertices by
\mathrm{NNP}(q,r)
, and write
L_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(q,r)
for the largest component inside
\Lambda_{n}
. We state a combination of two classical results
[
29
,
59
, Theorem 1.1]
that will replace
Claim

5.4
in this regime.

Proposition 5.5

(High-density NNP(
q,r
) has a large giant)
.

Consider nearest neighbor site-bond percolation
\mathrm{NNP}(q,r)
on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
with probabilities
q,r\in(0,1]
. For each
\varepsilon>0
, there exist constants
q_{0},r_{0}\in(0,1)
, and
A>0
such that for all
q\geq q_{0}
,
r\geq r_{0}
,
n\geq 1
,

\mathbb{P}\big(|L_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(q,r)|<(1-\varepsilon)n\big)\leq\exp\big(-\tfrac{1}{A}n^{(d-1)/d}\big).

Proof.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
. We shall define an
\varepsilon_{1}=\varepsilon_{1}(\varepsilon)
and set
q_{0}:=1-\varepsilon_{1}
and
r_{0}=1-\varepsilon_{1}
. Let
\omega\sim\mathrm{NNP}(q_{0},r_{0})
.
We couple
\omega
to iid Bernoulli bond percolation
\omega^{\star}\sim\mathrm{NNP}(1,1-12d\varepsilon_{1})
on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
, similarly to
[
7
,
59
]
. By a union bound over the endpoints of an edge and the edge itself, each edge in is open in
\omega
with
marginal
probability at least
1-3\varepsilon_{1}
. Consider an edge with endpoints
(x,y)
in
\omega
, and let
N_{(x,y)}:=\{\text{edges incident to $x$ or $y$ in ${\mathbb{Z}}^{d}$}\}\setminus\{(x,y)\}
. Clearly
|N_{(x,y)}|=4d-2
.
For any set of edges
S\subseteq N_{(x,y)}
, by a union bound,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}((x,y)\text{ open in }\omega\mid\text{all edges in $S$ open in $\omega$})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}((x,y)\text{ open}\text{ and }\text{all edges in $S$ open})

\displaystyle\geq 1-(4d-1)\cdot 3\varepsilon_{1}\geq 1-12d\varepsilon_{1}=:\tilde{p}.

Edges that do not share a vertex are independently open, so one can iterate this argument: for every finite set
E
of edges in
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
,
\mathbb{P}(\text{all edges in $E$ are open})\geq\tilde{p}^{|E|}
. Hence, by Strassen’s theorem
[
60
]
we can couple
\omega
with an independent bond percolation
\omega^{\star}
on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
with retention-probability
\tilde{p}
, where every open edge in
\omega^{\star}
is open in
\omega
.
Let us write
C_{B_{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\tilde{p})
for the largest component of
\omega^{\star}\cap\Lambda_{n}
. Given
\varepsilon>0
, by
[
29
, Theorem 1.1]
, there exists a
p_{\star}(\varepsilon)<1
so that whenever
\tilde{p}>p_{\star}(\varepsilon)
, for some
A>0
,

\mathbb{P}\big(|C_{B_{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\tilde{p})|<(1-\varepsilon)n\big)\leq\exp\big(-\tfrac{1}{A}n^{(d-1)/d}\big).

Hence, setting
\varepsilon_{1}>(1-p_{\star}(\varepsilon))/12d
yields
\tilde{p}>p(\varepsilon)
, and we obtain by the coupling
\omega\succcurlyeq\omega_{\star}
:

\mathbb{P}\big(|L_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(q,r)|<(1-\varepsilon)n\big)\leq\mathbb{P}\big(|C_{B_{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(\tilde{p})|<(1-\varepsilon)n\big)\leq\exp\big(-\tfrac{1}{A}n^{(d-1)/d}\big),

and the result follows for
q_{0}=r_{0}=1-\varepsilon_{1}
, and by monotonicity for all values
q\geq q_{0}
and
r\geq r_{0}
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

5.1
(short).

We give the proof for KSRGs on a Poisson point process. The result for long-range percolation on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
follows analogously to the case
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}>0
. By the assumptions in
Proposition

5.1
), there is a
\mathrm{type}\in\{\mathrm{hl},\mathrm{hh},\mathrm{ll}\}
so that
\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}>0
.
Positivity will ensure that “relatively long” edges are present to keep the giant together.
Let
M
be a sufficiently large constant. We assume for simplicity that
(n/M)^{1/d}\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Tessellate
\Lambda_{n}
into
m_{n}:=n/M
may boxes
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathcal{Q}}_{m_{n}}
of volume
M
. Similarly as before, we write
{\mathcal{V}}_{M,j}:={\mathcal{V}}\cap({\mathcal{Q}}_{j}\times[1,\infty))
for the vertices in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
,
{\mathcal{G}}_{M,j}
for the subgraph of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
induced by these vertices in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
, and pre-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
for the union of the subgraphs
{\mathcal{G}}_{M,j}
,
j\in[m_{n}]
.
Fix
\delta>0
small as in (
5.3
).

Good subboxes.
We call a box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j},j\in[m_{n}]

good
if the following event holds.
When
\mathrm{type}\in\{\mathrm{hl},\mathrm{hh}\}
, we set

(5.25)

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{box}}(j)\!:=\!\left\{\,\begin{aligned} \exists\mbox{comp. ${\mathcal{C}}_{j}$ in ${\mathcal{G}}_{M,j}\big[1,M^{1/(\tau-1)}\big)$}:|{\mathcal{C}}_{j}|&\geq\rho M,\\
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}&\supseteq{\mathcal{V}}_{M,j}[M^{(1-\delta)/(\tau-1)},M^{1/(\tau-1)})\\
\tfrac{1}{2}M^{\delta}&\leq|{\mathcal{V}}_{M,j}[M^{(1-\delta)/(\tau-1)},M^{1/(\tau-1)})|\end{aligned}\right\}.

When
\mathrm{type}=\mathrm{ll}
, we set

(5.26)

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{box}}(j):=\!\left\{\,\exists\mbox{comp. ${\mathcal{C}}_{j}$ in ${\mathcal{G}}_{M,j}\big[1,M^{1/(\tau-1)}\big)$}:|{\mathcal{C}}_{j}|\geq\rho M\right\}.

This event is measurable with respect to pre-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
. Our assumption (
5.3
) directly implies
Claim

5.3
, which in turn implies that the first two requirements in
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{box}}(j)
—concerning
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
— are satisfied with probability arbitrarily close to
1
by choosing
M
sufficiently large.
For the third requirement in (
5.25
) we use the intensity of the PPP (
4.5
), and compute for fixed
\delta>0

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{V}}_{M,j}[M^{(1-\delta)/(\tau-1)},M^{1/(\tau-1)})|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}M^{\delta}\big)\longrightarrow 1,

as
M
tends to infinity. Since the three conditions on the right-hand side hold with probability arbitrarily close to
1
, we may assume that
M
is so large that for a fixed
q\in(0,1)
and any
j\in[m_{n}]
, independently of all other boxes,

(5.27)

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{box}}(j)\big)>q.

The auxiliary graph and LDP for site-bond percolation.
Consider then the auxiliary graph
{\mathcal{H}}_{n}
, with vertex set
{\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}\subset[m_{n}]
. By definition of the boxing, the set of vertices
[m_{n}]
can be mapped to the integer points in a box of side-length
m_{n}^{1/d}
, such that any two adjacent boxes are mapped to integer points at distance one from each other.
We call
j\in{\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}
active if the box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
satisfies
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{box}}(j)
. Two active vertices
j,\ell\in{\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}
are connected in
{\mathcal{H}}_{n}
if they are neighboring vertices in
B_{m_{n}}
, and there exist vertices
v\in{\mathcal{C}}_{j},\tilde{v}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}
such that
v
is connected by an edge to
\tilde{v}
in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
. Let us write
{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
for the largest component in
{\mathcal{H}}_{n}
. Since each
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
in a good box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
has size at least
\rho n
, the event
\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|\geq(1-\varepsilon)m_{n}\}
implies
\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,M^{1/(\tau-1)})|\geq(1-\varepsilon)\rho n\}
.
Let
q,r>0
be such that by Proposition
5.5
for a fixed
\varepsilon>0
,
A>0
and all
n\geq 1
,

\mathbb{P}(|L_{m_{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(q,r)|<(1-\varepsilon)m_{n}\big)\leq\exp\big(-\tfrac{1}{A}m_{n}^{(d-1)/d}\big).

We will show that we can choose
M
sufficiently large (depending on
\varepsilon
) so that

(5.28)

{\mathcal{H}}_{n}\succcurlyeq\mathrm{NNP}(q,r).

This domination then implies that
|{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|\succcurlyeq|L_{m_{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(q,r)|
, and so

(5.29)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,M^{1/(\tau-1)})|<(1-\varepsilon)\rho n\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(|L_{m_{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}(q,r)|<(1-\varepsilon)m_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-\tfrac{1}{A}m_{n}^{(d-1)/d}\big)

\displaystyle=\exp\big(-\tfrac{1}{AM^{(d-1)/d}}n^{(d-1)/d}\big),

implying the statement of
Proposition

5.1
for a larger value
A
.

The auxiliary graph dominates site-bond percolation.
It remains to prove the domination (
5.28
).
By (
5.27
) and the definition of
{\mathcal{H}}_{n}
below (
5.27
), each vertex
j\in[m_{n}]
is independently
active
with probability at least
q
for
M
sufficiently large. Thus, it remains to show that for two large components
{\mathcal{C}}_{j},{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}
in adjacent good boxes
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\ell}
,

(5.30)

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{C}}_{j}\nsim_{{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}\mid\text{pre-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\big)=\prod_{u\in{\mathcal{C}}_{j},v\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}}(1-\mathrm{p}(u,v))\longrightarrow 0,\qquad\text{as }M\to\infty.

We write
|{\mathcal{E}}({\mathcal{C}}_{j},{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell})|
for the number of edges between the two components. Then

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{C}}_{j}\nsim_{{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}\mid\text{pre-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\big)\leq\exp\bigg(-\sum_{u\in{\mathcal{C}}_{j},v\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}}\mathrm{p}(u,v)\bigg)=\exp\big(-\mathbb{E}[|{\mathcal{E}}({\mathcal{C}}_{j},{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell})|\mid\text{pre-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}]\big).

We will show case-by-case for
\zeta\in\{\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}\}
that
\mathbb{E}[|{\mathcal{E}}({\mathcal{C}}_{j},{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell})|\mid\text{pre-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}]\to\infty
using either high-low, high-high, or low-low connections between two active neighboring boxes
j,\ell
. The maximal distance between any two vertices in neighboring boxes is
2\sqrt{d}M^{1/d}
.

1. Low-low connections help:
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}>0
.
We may assume
\alpha<\infty
otherwise
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}=2-\alpha
is negative. Every vertex has mark at least
1
. Thus, from the connection probability
\mathrm{p}
in (
4.6
) it follows that

(5.31)

\mathbb{E}[|{\mathcal{E}}({\mathcal{C}}_{j},{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell})|\mid\text{pre-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}]=\Omega\big(M^{-\alpha}|{\mathcal{C}}_{j}|\cdot|{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}|\big)=\Omega\big(M^{2-\alpha}\big).

Since by assumption
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}=2-\alpha>0
, (
5.30
) follows for fixed
\varepsilon
. The same calculation works for long-range percolation on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
.

2. High-low connections help:
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}>0
.

We may again assume
\alpha<\infty
otherwise
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}
is negative.
On the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{box}}(j)
, defined in (
5.25
), the component
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
contains at least
M^{\delta}/2
vertices with mark at least
M^{(1-\delta)/(\tau-1)}
.
Then, using possible edges between a high-mark vertex in
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
and the at least
\rho M
vertices in
{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}
that have mark at least
1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|{\mathcal{E}}({\mathcal{C}}_{j},{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell})|\mid\text{pre-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}]

\displaystyle=\Omega(M^{\delta+1})\cdot\Omega\bigg(1\wedge\Big(\frac{M^{(1-\delta)/(\tau-1)}}{M}\Big)^{\alpha}\bigg)

(5.32)

\displaystyle=\Omega(M^{1+\delta})\wedge\Omega(M^{1-\alpha(\tau-2)/(\tau-1)+\delta(1-\alpha/(\tau-1))}).

When
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}=1-(1-1/\alpha)(\tau-1)=(\tau-1)/\alpha-(\tau-2)>0
,
the exponent of
M
in (
5.32
) is positive for sufficiently small
\delta>0
. So (
5.30
) follows.

3. High-high connections help,
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}>0
.
We first assume that
\alpha<\infty
.
Using that we only consider good boxes, and that there are at least
M^{\delta}/2
many high-mark vertices with mark in the interval
[M^{(1-\delta)/(\tau-1)},M^{1/(\tau-1)})
in both
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell}
, we compute

\mathbb{E}[|{\mathcal{E}}({\mathcal{C}}_{j},{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell})|\mid\text{pre-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}]=\Omega(M^{2\delta})\cdot\Omega\bigg(1\wedge\Big(\frac{M^{(\sigma+1)(1-\delta)/(\tau-1)}}{M}\Big)^{\alpha}\bigg).

From (
2.14
),
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}>0
only when
\sigma>\tau-2
. So, for
\delta
is sufficiently small, the second factor is
\Theta(1)
. Thus, the right-hand side tends to infinity with
M
and (
5.30
) follows.
When
\alpha=\infty
, it follows analogously that
\mathbb{E}[|{\mathcal{E}}({\mathcal{C}}_{j},{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell})|\mid\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}]=\Omega(M^{2\delta})
if
\delta
is sufficiently small since the minimum is attained at
1
on the right-hand side above.

Conclusion.
We conclude that (
5.30
) follows in all three cases, proving the stochastic domination (
5.28
) and finishing the proof of (
5.5
).
∎

5.3.
Verification of the requirement

We now verify the requirement (
5.3
). To do so, we prove the following lemma that we only need for models with non-trivial vertex marks.
We define one more exponent for each regime
\mathrm{hl,ll,hh}
:

(5.33)

\eta_{\mathrm{hl}}:=1-\gamma_{\mathrm{hl}}(\tau-1)/\alpha,\qquad\eta_{\mathrm{ll}}:=1/\alpha,\qquad\eta_{\mathrm{hh}}:=1-\sigma\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}.

Using the respective values of
\gamma_{\mathrm{ll}},\gamma_{\mathrm{hl}},\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}
in (
5.2
), (
2.11
), and (
2.13
), it is elementary to verify that if
\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}=\zeta_{\star}
for some
\mathrm{type}\in\{\mathrm{ll},\mathrm{hl},\mathrm{hh}\}
, then
\eta_{\mathrm{type}}>\gamma_{\mathrm{type}}
holds (see the proof of
Lemma

5.6
). Then there are values of
\alpha
such that
\gamma_{\mathrm{ll}}>\eta_{\mathrm{ll}}
.
We write
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)[1,w)
for the component of the origin spanned on vertices in
{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[1,w)
.

Lemma 5.6

(Poly-logarithmic mark-thresholds)
.

Consider a supercritical KSRG satisfying Assumption
2.2
on a PPP. Assume for a
\mathrm{type}\in\{\mathrm{ll},\mathrm{hl}\}
that
\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}=\max(\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}},\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}})>0
.
Whenever there exist constants
\delta\in[0,\zeta)
,
A^{\prime},\rho>0
, such that for all
n\geq 1
,

(5.34)

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A^{\prime}n^{\gamma_{\mathrm{type}}})|<\rho n\big)\leq\exp\big(-\tfrac{1}{A^{\prime}}n^{\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}-\delta}\big),

then, for all
C>0
, there exists a constant
A>0
such that for all
n
sufficiently large,

(5.35)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\big|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big[1,(A\log n)^{\gamma_{\mathrm{type}}/(\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}-\delta)}\big)\big|\geq\rho n\big)

\displaystyle\geq 1-n^{-C},

and

(5.36)

\mathbb{P}\big(\exists\mbox{ comp. }{\mathcal{C}}\mbox{ in }{\mathcal{G}}_{n}:{\mathcal{C}}\supseteq{\mathcal{V}}\big[(A\log n)^{\eta_{\mathrm{type}}/(\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}-\delta)},\infty\big),|{\mathcal{C}}|\geq\rho n\big)\geq 1-n^{-C}.

The statements remain valid for the Palm version
\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}
of
\mathbb{P}
for any
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.

The proof is an adaptation of
[
48
, Proposition 5.12]
and we present it in Appendix
B.3
.
In a nutshell, we apply a boxing scheme —with box sizes
\Theta((\log n)^{1/(\zeta-\delta)})
— and apply (
5.34
) to each box. To form
{\mathcal{C}}
, we connect boxes to each other using high- and/or low-mark vertices depending on the regime. Then, we connect vertices of mark at least
(A\log n)^{\eta/(\zeta-\delta)}
to the constant-mark vertices in
{\mathcal{C}}
. Computations are similar to those in
Section

6.1
.

The corollaries of

Propositions

5.1
and
5.6
motivate the somewhat counterintuitive formulation of
Lemma

5.6
, i.e., the requirement (
5.34
) with both
\delta>0
and
\delta=0
allowed.

Corollary 5.7

(A giant with sharper probability)
.

Consider a supercritical KSRG satisfying Assumption
2.2
on a PPP or consider supercritical long-range percolation on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
. Assume that
\zeta_{\mathrm{long}}>0
, and
\zeta_{\star}=\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}
for some
\mathrm{type}\in\{\mathrm{ll},\mathrm{hl},\mathrm{hh},\mathrm{short}\}
. Set
\gamma_{\mathrm{short}}:=0
. There exist constants
A_{1}>0
such that for all
n\geq 1
,

(5.37)

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A_{1}n^{\gamma_{\mathrm{type}}})|<\tfrac{1}{A_{1}}n\big)\leq\exp\big(-\tfrac{1}{A_{1}}n^{\max(\zeta_{\mathrm{long}},(d-1)/d)}\big).

Proof.

We first consider KSRGs on a PPP. By
Claim

2.3
,
\zeta_{\mathrm{long}}=\max(\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}})
.
When
\zeta_{\mathrm{long}}\in\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}\}
, Proposition
4.1
proves the requirement (
5.34
) of
Lemma

5.6
for some
\delta>0
and a small value of
\rho>0
. Hence, (
5.35
)-(
5.36
) holds for these values of
\delta
and
\rho
. This implies the requirement (
5.3
) of
Proposition

5.1
. When
\zeta_{\mathrm{\star}}=\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}>0
, (
5.3
) follows from the combination of the law of large numbers
[
48
, Corollary 2.3]
, and that
|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|
contains all vertices of at least some polylogarithmic weight in
n
by
[
48
, Proposition 5.12]
, which then gives (
5.34
) and thus Lemma
5.6
. This proves (
5.37
) when
\zeta_{\star}\in\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}\}
, and gives the requirement (
5.3
) of
Proposition

5.1
whenever
\zeta_{\mathrm{long}}>0
. Further, the case for
\zeta_{\star}=\zeta_{\mathrm{short}}
follows from (
5.5
).
For long-range percolation on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
,
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}
and
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}
are negative, so
\zeta_{\mathrm{long}}=\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}=2-\alpha
. The requirement (
5.34
) of
Lemma

5.6
is verified by
[
14
, Theorem 3.2]
.
∎

The actual mark-threshold so that
all
vertices above that mark are in the giant is as follows.

Corollary 5.8

(Poly-logarithmic mark-thresholds)
.

Consider a supercritical KSRG satisfying Assumption
2.2
on a PPP or consider long-range percolation on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
. Assume
\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}=\max(\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}})>0
for some
\mathrm{type}\in\{\mathrm{ll},\mathrm{hl}\}
.
For all
C>0
, there exist constants
A,\varepsilon>0
such that for all
n
sufficiently large,

(5.38)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\big|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big[1,(A\log n)^{\gamma_{\mathrm{type}}/\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}}\big)\big|\geq\varepsilon n\big)

\displaystyle\geq 1-n^{-C},

and

(5.39)

\mathbb{P}\big(\exists\mbox{ comp. }{\mathcal{C}}\mbox{ in }{\mathcal{G}}_{n}:{\mathcal{C}}\supseteq{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\big[(A\log n)^{\eta_{\mathrm{type}}/\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}},\infty\big),|{\mathcal{C}}|\geq\varepsilon n\big)\geq 1-n^{-C}.

Proof.

In
Corollary

5.7
we have just shown that the requirement (
5.34
) in
Lemma

5.6
is met with
\delta=0
for the case
\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}=\max(\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}})
.
This yields the first two bounds (
5.38
)–(
5.39
) for KSRGs on a PPP. In long-range percolation, all marks are equal to one, so that
{\mathcal{V}}[(A\log n)^{\eta_{\mathrm{type}}/\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}},\infty)=\emptyset
, and the two bounds are implied by
Corollary

5.7
.∎

In
Corollary

5.7
we obtain the sharp decay on the probability of having a giant, i.e., we obtain the target decay of the lower tail in
Theorem

2.7
. However, the proportion of vertices in the giant is not yet close to
\theta
.

6.
Lower tail of large deviations

In this section we prove
Theorem

2.7
. We first show uniqueness of the giant via an upper bound on the size of the second-largest component
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
. In
Section

6.2
, we extend this upper bound on
|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|
to the upper bound on the cluster-size decay in
Theorem

2.9
using our previous work
[
48
]
. A Law of Large Numbers for
|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|
will follow. Eventually we prove the lower tail of large deviations.

6.1.
Non-giant components are small

We prove an upper bound on
|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|
when
\max(\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}},\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}})>0
. When
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}>0
, we proved a similar upper bound in
[
48
]
using different techniques, see
Remark

3.2
. See also
Remark

3.1
on the difficulties one needs to overcome to extend this result to the case when surface tension dominates connections, i.e., when
\zeta_{\star}=\zeta_{\mathrm{short}}
.

Proposition 6.1

(Upper bound second-largest component)
.

Consider a supercritical KSRG on a PPP or supercritical long-range percolation on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
. Assume
\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}=\max(\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}})>0
for some
\mathrm{type}\in\{\mathrm{ll},\mathrm{hl}\}
. There exists a constant
A>0
such that for all
n>k
,

(6.1)

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|>k\big)\leq(n/k)\exp\big(-\tfrac{1}{A}k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}}\big).

The statement remains valid for the Palm version
\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}
of
\mathbb{P}
for any
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.

The proof of Proposition
6.1
is inspired by Sly and Crawford
[
24
]
who obtain a polylogarithmic upper bound with unidentified exponent on the second-largest component for long-range percolation. Contrary to long-range percolation, we have to use the vertex-marks as well when
\tau<\infty
.
We present the proof for KSRGs on Poisson point processes satisfying
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}>0
, and explain the needed changes for
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}>0
and for KSRGs on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
at the end.
We abbreviate
\gamma=\gamma_{\mathrm{hl}}=1-1/\alpha
and
\zeta=\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}=1-\gamma_{\mathrm{hl}}(\tau-1)=(\tau-1)/\alpha-(\tau-2)
. Clearly
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}>0
implies
\alpha<\infty
.

We let
A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}
be the constant from (
5.37
) in
Corollary

5.7
so that for all
n\geq 1
,

(6.2)

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}n^{\gamma})|\leq\tfrac{1}{A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}}n\big)\leq\exp\big(-\tfrac{1}{A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}}n^{\zeta}\big).

For simplicity we assume that
(n/k)^{1/d}\in{\mathbb{N}}
, so that
\Lambda_{n}
can be partitioned into volume-
2^{-d}k
boxes.

Definition 6.2

(Boxing scheme)
.

Fix
k\geq 1
sufficiently large. Consider the boxes
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathcal{Q}}_{2^{d}n/k}
that partition
\Lambda_{n}
, and that are labeled so that
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
is adjacent to
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j+1}
for all
j\in[2^{d}n/k-1]
. We write
{\mathcal{V}}_{k,j}
for the PPP restricted to the box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
,
{\mathcal{G}}_{k,j}
for the graph
{\mathcal{G}}
restricted to
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
.
We call vertices with mark respectively below and at least
A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma}
low-mark and high-mark vertices. We call

{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma})
the local low-largest component of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
. We also define

(6.3)

{\mathcal{U}}_{j}:=\{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{k,j}[A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma},\infty):v\sim{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma})\}.

We then define the local giant component of box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
as

(6.4)

\mathrm{LG}_{j}:={\mathcal{U}}_{j}\cup{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma}).

In words,
{\mathcal{U}}_{j}
is the set of high-mark vertices in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
that connect by at least one edge to the local low-largest component in the same box. So, together they form the local giant component
\mathrm{LG}_{j}
.
We mention that
{\mathcal{U}}_{j}
typically does not contain all high-mark vertices.

Definition 6.3

(Revealment stages)
.

Consider a KSRG under the same setting as Proposition
6.1
such that
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}>0
, with the boxing scheme from
Definition

6.2
. Initially, we reveal the realization of vertex set
{\mathcal{V}}
, and none of the edges. We define the following four edge-revealment stages:

(1)

Reveal edges between pairs of vertices both in the same box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
.

(2)

Reveal edges between pairs of vertices both in
\cup_{j\leq 2^{d}(n/k)}\mathrm{LG}_{j}
.

(3)

Reveal remaining edges between pairs of vertices both in
{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\setminus\big(\cup_{j\leq 2^{d}(n/k)}\mathrm{LG}_{j}\big)

(4)

Reveal all remaining edges.

We write
stage-
j
-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
for the graph obtained after the
j
th revealment stage for
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
.

Thus, in stage
(
1
)
we reveal all ‘local’ edges between vertices inside the same box. In stage
(
2
)
, edges between local giants of different boxes. In stage
(
3
)
, we reveal edges with both endpoints outside local giants in different boxes. Finally, in stage
(
4
)
we reveal all remaining edges: edges in different boxes where one endpoint is in a local giant, and the other endpoint is outside a local giant. The set
\mathrm{LG}_{j}
, (the local-low largest component and high-mark vertices connecting to it in the same box) is determined during stage
(
1
)
. So stages
(
2
–
4
)
are well-defined. These latter stages reveal edges between
different
boxes.

Definition 6.4

(Stage​
(1)
and
(2)
good events)
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1/(2^{d}A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}))
be a small constant.
We call a box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
1-good if it satisfies the event

(6.5)

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{1\textnormal{-}good}}({\mathcal{Q}}_{j}):=\{{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}\ 1\text{-good}\}:=\big\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma})|>\delta k\big\}\cap\big\{|{\mathcal{U}}_{j}|>\delta k^{\zeta}\big\}.

For
j<2^{d}(n/k)
, we call
u\in{\mathcal{U}}_{j}
box-wedging if
u\sim\mathrm{LG}_{j+1}
.
We say that stage-2-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
is
2
-good if

(6.6)

\{\text{stage-2-${\mathcal{G}}_{n}$ 2-good}\}:=\bigcap_{j\leq 2^{d}(n/k)}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{1\textnormal{-}good}}({\mathcal{Q}}_{j})\cap\bigcap_{j<2^{d}(n/k)}\big\{\exists u\in{\mathcal{U}}_{j}:u\sim\mathrm{LG}_{j+1}\big)\big\}.

Whether
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
is
1
-good is entirely determined by the revealed edges in stage
(1)
, and whether stage-
2
-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
is
2
-good, is entirely determined by edges revealed by the end of stage
(2)
. The second intersection in (
6.6
)
means that there is at least one box-wedging vertex in each local giant, implying that all local giants are part of the same component in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
that we will call the backbone.

Claim 6.5

(Stage
(
1
)
error bound)
.

Consider a KSRG under the same setting as Proposition
6.1
such that
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}>0
. There exists a constant
\varepsilon>0
such that for all
k
sufficiently large, for all
j\leq 2^{d}(n/k)
,

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{Q}}_{j}\mbox{ is 1-good}\big)\geq 1-\exp(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta}).

Proof.

We estimate the probabilities of the two events in (
6.5
). By
Corollary

5.7
,

(6.7)

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}[1,A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma})\leq\delta k\big)\leq\exp\big(-2^{-d\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}/A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}\big).

Next, consider
v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma},\infty)
in box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
.
We use that each vertex in
{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}[1,A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma})
has mark at least
1
, and that the distance between any two vertices in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
is at most
\sqrt{d}k^{1/d}
. Thus, by the connection probability
\mathrm{p}
in (
4.6
) (with
\alpha<\infty
), conditional on the vertex set
{\mathcal{V}}_{n}
and the subgraph
{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}[1,A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma})
having size at least
\delta k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(v\nsim{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}[1,A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma})\,\big|\,|{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}[1,A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma})|

\displaystyle\geq\delta k,{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\big)\leq\Big(1-p\Big(1\wedge\beta A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}\frac{k^{\gamma}}{d^{d/2}k}\Big)^{\alpha}\Big)^{\delta k}.

If the minimum is at
1
, then this probability is at most
e^{-p\delta k}
. If the minimum is at the second expression, we substitute
\gamma=(\alpha-1)/\alpha
. When we bound
1-x\leq e^{-x}
, the powers of
k
cancel, and the right-hand side is at most

(6.8)

\mathbb{P}\big(v\notin{\mathcal{U}}_{j}\mid|{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}[1,A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma})|\geq\delta k,{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\big)\leq\exp(-p\beta A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}^{\alpha}/d^{d/2}).

In both cases, there is
\delta_{1}>0
such that any such
v
connects by an edge to
{\mathcal{C}}_{k,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma})
with probability at least
\delta_{1}
, independently of all other vertices.
Now,
{\mathcal{V}}_{k,j}\cap[A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma},\infty)
is by (
4.5
) a PPP with intensity
\mathrm{Leb}\times F_{W}(\mathrm{d}w)
. Therefore,
the vertices in
{\mathcal{V}}_{k,1}\cap[A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma},\infty)
that connect by an edge to
{\mathcal{C}}_{k,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma})
dominate a Poisson random variable with mean

\delta_{1}\cdot\mathrm{Vol}({\mathcal{Q}}_{j})(A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma})^{-(\tau-1)}=\delta_{1}2^{-d}A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}^{-(\tau-1)}k^{1-\gamma(\tau-1)}=\Theta(k^{\zeta}).

Thus, by
Lemma

A.1
, there exists
\delta=\delta(A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}})>0
small enough so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{U}}_{j}|\leq\delta k\,\big|\,|{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}[1,A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma})|\geq\delta k\big)\leq\exp(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta}).

Combined with (
6.7
) and the definition of
\{{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\mbox{ is $1$-good}\}
in
Definition

6.4
this finishes the proof for
k
sufficiently large and
\delta,\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small.
∎

Claim 6.6

(Stage
(
2
)
error bound)
.

Consider a KSRG under the same setting as Proposition
6.1
such that
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}>0
. There exists a constant
\varepsilon>0
such that for all
k
sufficiently large,

\mathbb{P}\big(\mbox{stage-2-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\mbox{ is 2-good}\big)\geq 1-2^{d+1}(n/k)\exp(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta}).

Proof.

By
Claim

6.5
, each box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
is
1
-good with probability at least
1-\exp(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta})
. Denote the first event in (
6.6
) that all boxes
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
are
1
-good by
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}
. By a union bound,

(6.9)

\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}})\geq 1-2^{d}(n/k)\exp(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta}).

We move on to estimating the probability of the second event in (
6.6
) conditioned on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}
. Fix some
j<2^{d}(n/k)
. Before Stage
(
2
)
, none of the edges of
{\mathcal{U}}_{j}
towards vertices in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j+1}
have been revealed yet. The maximal Euclidean distance between two neighboring boxes is
2\sqrt{d}k^{1/d}/2
, so we can bound the probability that a single high-mark vertex is box-wedging using (
4.6
). When
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j+1}
is
1
-good, by (
6.5
) there are at least
\delta n
vertices of mark at least
1
in
\mathrm{LG}_{j+1}
, so

(6.10)

\mathbb{P}\big(u\in{\mathcal{U}}_{j}\text{ not box-wedging }\big|\,{\mathcal{Q}}_{j+1}\mbox{ $1$-good},{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\big)\leq\Big(1-p\Big(1\wedge\frac{\beta A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma}}{d^{d/2}k}\Big)^{\alpha}\Big)^{\delta k}.

Then, we can use that there are at least
\delta k^{\zeta}
candidate vertices to be box-wedging in a
1
-good
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
, and that edges are present independently given the vertex set
{\mathcal{V}}
. Thus,

(6.11)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\nexists u\in{\mathcal{U}}_{j}:u\text{ box-wedging}\,\big|\,{\mathcal{Q}}_{j},{\mathcal{Q}}_{j+1}\mbox{ $1$-good},{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1-p\Big(1\wedge\frac{\beta A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}k^{\gamma}}{d^{d/2}k}\Big)^{\alpha}\Big)^{(\delta k)\cdot(\delta k^{\zeta})}\hskip-6.0pt.

If the minimum on the right-hand side is at
1
, this probability is at most
\exp(-\varepsilon k^{1+\zeta})
. If the minimum is at the second expression, using that
1-x\leq\exp(-x)
and
\gamma=1-1/\alpha
, the powers of
k
simplify since
(k^{\gamma}/k)^{\alpha}\cdot k=1
, so this probability is at most

\exp(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta})

for
\varepsilon>0
small enough.
So, integrating over the realizations
{\mathcal{V}}_{n}
and a union bound over all
2^{d}(n/k)
boxes, the probability that there is at least one box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
with no box-wedging vertices is at most
2^{d}(n/k)\exp(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta})
. Combined with (
6.6
) and (
6.9
) the claim follows.
∎

Claim 6.7

(Stage
(
3
–
4
)
error bound: second-largest component)
.

Consider a KSRG under the same setting as Proposition
6.1
such that
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}>0
. There exists a constant
\varepsilon>0
such that for all
k
sufficiently large and
n>k

(6.12)

\mathbb{P}\big(\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|>k\}\cap\mbox{stage-2-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\mbox{ is 2-good}\big)\leq(n/k)\exp(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta}).

Proof.

We condition on the full realization of the stage-
2
-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
that is
2
-good.
By (
6.6
), any such realization satisfies that all local giants
(\mathrm{LG}_{j})_{j\leq 2^{d}(n/k)}
have size at least
\delta k
and they are all connected by box-wedging vertices. This gives a component in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
of size at least
\delta n
that contains these local giants. We call this component the backbone
\mathrm{BB}=\mathrm{BB}_{n,k}
.
In Stage
(
3
)
we expose all edges with both endpoints outside the local giants. If no component of size larger than
k
except for the backbone remains after Stage
(
3
)
, then we are done: In stage
(
4
)
all remaining edges to be revealed have one endpoint in local giants, hence in
\mathrm{BB}
, and one outside.

We may interpret now (
6.12
) as follows: the second-largest component being larger than
k
means that there is at least one component above size
k
that is not
\mathrm{BB}
.
If after Stage
(
3
)
there is a component
{\mathcal{C}}\neq\mathrm{BB}
above size
k
, then we aim to show that this component merges with
\mathrm{BB}
in Stage
(
4
)
with probability at least
1-\exp(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta})
. Since there are at most
(n/k)
components above size
k
after Stage
(
3
)
, a union bound over all such components will yield (
6.12
).

So, suppose that there exists a component
{\mathcal{C}}\neq\mathrm{BB}
of size larger than
k
after Stage
(
3
)
and fix a realization of stage-
2
-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
. After Stage
(
3
)
we revealed already all within-box edges, but we did not reveal edges between
{\mathcal{C}}
and local giants in neighboring boxes ‘around’ vertices of
{\mathcal{C}}
. Since the number of boxes is at least
2^{d}(n/k)
by
Definition

6.2
, a subbox always has a neighboring box. Let
j(v)
denote index of the box that vertex
v\in{\mathcal{C}}
belongs to, which is a.s. unique.
Since all boxes are
1
-good, by (
6.5
), for each
v\in{\mathcal{C}}
, there are at least
\delta k^{\zeta}
high-mark vertices at distance at most
\sqrt{d}k^{1/d}
from
v
in the vertex set
{\mathcal{U}}_{j(v)+1}\subseteq\mathrm{LG}_{j(v)+1}
in the neighboring boxes
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j(v)-1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j(v)+1}
. Each vertex of
v
has mark at least
1
. Hence, for a given component
{\mathcal{C}}
, by the same calculations as in the proof of
Claim

6.6
around (
6.10
)–(
6.11
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{C}}

\displaystyle\nsim\mathrm{BB}\,\big|\,\mbox{2-good-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n},,{\mathcal{V}}_{n},\mbox{stage-3-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\big)\leq\Big(1-p\Big(1\wedge\beta A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}\frac{k^{\gamma}}{d^{d/2}k}\Big)^{\alpha}\Big)^{k\cdot(\delta k^{\zeta})}\leq\exp\big(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta}\big).

for
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small. Here we used that
\gamma=1-1/\alpha
and thus some powers of
k
cancel.
By a union bound over all at most
|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}|/k
components of size at least
k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|>k\}\cap\{\mbox{stage-2-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\mbox{ is 2-good}\}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\geq k\,\big|\,\mbox{2-good-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n},{\mathcal{V}}_{n},\mbox{stage-3-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\big)\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}|/k]\exp\big(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta}\big)\leq(n/k)\exp\big(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta}\big).

This finishes the proof of (
6.12
).
∎

We are ready to prove Proposition
6.1
that bounds
\mathbb{P}(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\geq k)
.

Proof of Proposition
6.1
when
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}>0
.

By the law of total probability,

\mathbb{P}(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\geq k)\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\geq k\}\cap\mbox{stage-2-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\mbox{ is 2-good}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(\mbox{stage-2-}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\mbox{ is \emph{not} 2-good}\big).

Proposition
6.1
follows from Claims
6.6
–
6.7
.
∎

We now give a sketch on how to modify the above proof when
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}>0
.

Proof of Proposition
6.1
when
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}>0
.

The proofs are identical for KSRGs on PPPs and on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
.
We follow the
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}
-computations.
By
Corollary

5.7
,

(6.13)

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big[1,A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}})|\leq\tfrac{1}{A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}}n\big)\leq\exp\big(-\tfrac{1}{A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}}}n^{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}}\big).

We set
\mathrm{LG}_{j}:={\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big[1,A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}})
to be the local giant of box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
. We ignore high-mark vertices here.
The revealment scheme in
Definition

6.3
is then well-defined with this
\mathrm{LG}_{j}
. We modify the box being
1
-good in (
6.5
) by only requesting the first event
|\mathrm{LG}_{j}|=|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big[1,A_{\ref{cor:initial-upper}})|\geq\delta k
there. We define
u\in\mathrm{LG}_{j}
to be box-wedging if
u\sim\mathrm{LG}_{j+1}
.
In the definition of the stage
2
-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
being
2
-good in (
6.6
), we then require at least one box-wedging vertex in each box:

\bigcap_{j<2^{d}(n/k)}\{\exists u\in\mathrm{LG}_{j}:u\sim\mathrm{LG}_{j+1}\}.

Claim

6.5
follows then from
Corollary

5.7
as before. We describe how to prove the equivalent of
Claim

6.6
for this regime. Estimating that all boxes are
1
-good goes as before in (
6.9
). For the second part, estimating that each box has a box-wedging vertex, we use only the fact that vertices have weight at least
1
. We get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\nexists u\in\mathrm{LG}_{j}:u\sim\mathrm{LG}_{j+1}\,

\displaystyle\big|\,{\mathcal{Q}}_{j},{\mathcal{Q}}_{j+1}\mbox{ are $1$-good}\big)\leq\Big(1-p\Big(1\wedge\beta\frac{1}{d^{d/2}k}\Big)^{\alpha}\Big)^{(\delta k)^{2}}.

If the first term is the minimum, this probability is at most
\exp(-\varepsilon k^{2})\leq\exp(-\varepsilon k^{2-\alpha})=\exp(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}})
for
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small. If the second term is the minimum, this probability is also at most
\exp(-\varepsilon k^{2-\alpha})=\exp(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}})
. The rest of the claim is as for the hl-regime. For
Claim

6.7
, we use again that each local giant contains at least
\delta k
vertices of mark at least
1
. Thus,

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{C}}\nsim\mathrm{BB}\,\big|\,\mbox{2-good-${\mathcal{G}}_{n}$},{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\big)\leq\Big(1-p\Big(1\wedge\beta\frac{1}{d^{d/2}k}\Big)^{\alpha}\Big)^{(\delta k)\cdot k}.

As before, the right-hand side is at most
\exp(-\varepsilon k^{2-\alpha})=\exp(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}})
. The proof of the claim is finished as before.
∎

We move towards the proofs of
Theorem

2.10
. We recall a proposition from our recent paper to prove the lower bounds.
To make notation lighter, we state it only for the case that
\zeta_{\mathrm{long}}>0
. We recall from (
2.16
) that
\max({\mathcal{Z}})
is the maximum and
\mathfrak{m}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}
is the multiplicity of the maximum in the set
{\mathcal{Z}}=\{\zeta_{\mathrm{short}},\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}\}
. We write here
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)[1,A)
for the component of a vertex with unknown weight at
0\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
inside the vertex set on
\Lambda_{n}\times[1,A)
, with the idea that if
w_{0}>A
then
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)[1,A):=\emptyset
.

Proposition 6.8

(Lower bound prerequisite
[
48
, Prop. 7.1]
)
.

Consider a supercritical KSRG on a PPP or on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
satisfying Assumption
2.2
with
\zeta_{\mathrm{long}}>0
. Assume that there exist constants
\eta^{\prime},\rho>0
such that for all
n
sufficiently large,

(6.14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(\big|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)\big[1,(\log n)^{\eta^{\prime}}\big)\big|\geq\rho n\big)\geq\rho.

Then there exists
A>0
such that for all
n\in[Ak,\infty]
,

(6.15)

\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)|>k,0\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)\geq\exp\big(-Ak^{\max({\mathcal{Z}})}(\log k)^{\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{Z}}-1}\big).

Moreover, there exist
\delta,\varepsilon>0
, such that for all
n
sufficiently large,

(6.16)

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|>\big((\varepsilon\log n)/(\log\log n)^{\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{Z}}-1}\big)^{1/\max({\mathcal{Z}})}\big)\geq 1-n^{-\delta}.

The proof of this proposition can be found in
[
48
, Section 7]
. The key idea is to find a localized component containing
0
in the ball of radius
\Theta(k)
around
0
, simultaneously ensuring that no edges leave this ball. The probabilistic cost of this event gives the lower bound on the cluster size-decay. The lower bound on the second-largest component can be derived from there, using a tesselation of the original space with boxes of volume
\Theta(n^{\varepsilon})
. Each tile corresponds to a repeated trial of the local event, and we prove that the small balls inside the tiles do not connect to other tiles with sufficiently high probability. See
[
48
, Section 7.3]
for more details.
The next claim verifies the requirement (
6.14
) for applying
Proposition

6.8
.

Claim 6.9

(The prerequisite holds)
.

Consider a KSRG under the same setting as
Theorem

2.10
and
Theorem

2.9
. There exist constants
\eta^{\prime},\rho>0
such that

(6.17)

\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)[1,(\log n)^{\eta^{\prime}}|\geq\rho n\big)\geq\rho.

Proof.

We prove the bound (
6.17
) for KSRGs on a Poisson point process and leave it to the reader to adapt it to long-range percolation.
We rely on Corollary
5.8
. Fix
(\zeta,\gamma)\in\{(\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}},\gamma_{\mathrm{hl}}),(\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\gamma_{\mathrm{ll}})\}
such that
\zeta>0
.
Let
A,\varepsilon>0
be so that (
5.38
) holds with
C=2
.
For any
u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}
, let
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)
be the component of
u
in the induced graph in
\Lambda_{n}\times[1,(A\log n)^{\gamma/\zeta})
; and let
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{2^{d}n,u}(u)
be the connected component of
u
in
\Lambda_{2^{d}n}(u)\times[1,(A\log n)^{\gamma/\zeta})
. Since
\Lambda_{n}\subseteq\Lambda_{2^{d}n}(u)
, clearly
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{2^{d}n,u}(u)
.
If the giant of
\Lambda_{n}\times[1,(A\log n)^{\gamma/\zeta})
has size at least
\varepsilon n
, then there are at least
\varepsilon n
vertices in a component of size at least
\varepsilon n
in
\Lambda_{n}\times[1,(A\log n)^{\gamma/\zeta})
. This happens with probability at least
1-n^{-2}
by (
5.38
). So,

(6.18)

(1-n^{-2})\varepsilon n\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}}\mathds{1}_{\{|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)|\geq\varepsilon n\}}\bigg]\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}}\mathds{1}_{\{|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{2^{d}n,u}(u)|\geq\varepsilon n\}}\bigg].

We now show that

(6.19)

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}}\mathds{1}_{\{\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{2^{d}n,u}(u)|\geq\varepsilon n\}}\bigg]=n\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{C}}_{2^{d}n}(0)[1,(A\log n)^{\gamma/\zeta})|\geq\varepsilon n\big).

For this, we use an alternative KSRG construction. Consider the collection
\bm{U}=(U_{ij})_{j>i\geq 1}
of independent
\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]
random variables, independent of the PPP
{\mathcal{V}}_{n}
. We can use
\bm{U}
to encode the presence of edges: given the PPP
{\mathcal{V}}
, order the points with respect to their distance to the origin, and include an edge between the
i
th and
j
th vertex
u_{i}
and
u_{j}
if
U_{ij}\leq\mathrm{p}(u_{i},u_{j})
. The function
\mathds{1}_{\{|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{2^{d}n,u}|\geq\varepsilon n\}}
is determined by the realization of the PPP
{\mathcal{V}}_{n}
and
\bm{U}
(i.e., it is measurable w.r.t. the sigma-algebras generated by these processes/collections). By Mecke’s formula and Fubini’s theorem,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}}\mathds{1}_{\{\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{2^{d}n,u}(u)|\geq\varepsilon n\}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{u\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}}\mathds{1}_{\{\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{2^{d}n,u}(u)|\geq\varepsilon n\}}\mid\bm{U}\bigg]\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{x\in\Lambda_{n}}\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{2^{d}n,u}(u)|\geq\varepsilon n\mid\bm{U},x_{u}=x\big)\mathrm{d}x\bigg]

\displaystyle=\int_{x\in\Lambda_{n}}\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{2^{d}n,u}(u)|\geq\varepsilon n\mid x_{u}=x\big)\mathrm{d}x.

The box
\Lambda_{2^{d}n}(u)
is centered at
u
. So, by translation invariance, the integrand is constant and equal to
\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{2^{d}n}(0)|\geq\varepsilon n\big)
. Since
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{2^{d}n}(0)={\mathcal{C}}_{2^{d}n}(0)[1,(A\log n)^{\gamma/\zeta})
by definition above (
6.18
), this proves (
6.19
). Combined with (
6.18
), this proves (
6.17
) for any
\eta^{\prime}>\gamma/\zeta
,
\rho=2^{-d}\varepsilon
, and any
n
sufficiently large. The statement is trivial for smaller
n
by decreasing
\rho
, finishing the proof.
∎

We are ready to prove
Theorem

2.10
on the second-largest component.

Proof of
Theorem

2.10
.

We give the proof for KSRGs on PPPs only: the proof for long-range percolation
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
is identical to the proof of the
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}
-regime for KSRGs. Assume
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}
or
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}
is strictly positive and maximal in
{\mathcal{Z}}=\{\zeta_{\mathrm{short}},\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}\}
. The lower bound holds by Proposition
6.8
, since its prerequisite (
6.14
) is satisfied by
Claim

6.9
.
The upper bound follows from Proposition
6.1
, substituting
k=k_{n}=(A\log n)^{1/\max(\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}},\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}})}
for a large constant
A=A(\delta)>0
.
∎

6.2.
Cluster-size decay and law of large numbers

We proceed to the proof of
Theorem

2.9
on the cluster-size decay. The upper bound (
6.1
) is the main prerequisite (
6.20
) of the following general proposition. We proved this proposition for KSRGs in general in
[
48
]
. We recall that
\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}
is the Palm measure of the PPP with a vertex at
x
.

Proposition 6.10

(Prerequisites for cluster-size decay
[
48
, Proposition 6.1]
)
.

Consider a supercritical KSRG on a PPP or on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
satisfying Assumption
2.2
. Assume that there exist
\zeta
,
\eta^{\prime}
,
c
,
c^{\prime}
,
M_{c}>0
, and a function
n_{0}(k)=O(k^{1+c^{\prime}})
such that for all
k
sufficiently large and whenever
n\in[n_{0}(k),\infty)
, with
\overline{w}(n,c):=M_{c}(\log n)^{\eta^{\prime}}
it holds for all
x\in\Lambda_{n}
,

(6.20)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|>k\big)

\displaystyle\leq n^{c^{\prime}}\exp\big(-ck^{\zeta}\big),

(6.21)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|<n^{c}\big)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1-c},

(6.22)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(\exists v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[\overline{w}(n,c),\infty):v\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-c}.

Then there exists a constant
A>0
such that for all
k
sufficiently large and
n\in[n_{0}(k),\infty]
,

(6.23)

\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)|>k,0\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)\leq\exp\big(-\tfrac{1}{A}k^{\zeta}\big),

and

(6.24)

\frac{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|}{n}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\theta(\beta,p,\alpha,\tau,\sigma),\qquad\mbox{as }n\to\infty.

In our current setting, (
6.1
) in Proposition
6.1
proves the first prerequisite and Corollary
5.8
proves the second, and implies the third prerequisite. We spell out the details. This is similar to
[
48
, Proposition 5.12]
.

Claim 6.11
.

Consider a supercritical KSRG on a PPP satisfying Assumption
2.2
or consider supercritical long-range percolation on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
. Assume
\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}=\max(\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}})>0
for some
\mathrm{type}\in\{\mathrm{ll},\mathrm{hl}\}
. For each constant
C>0
, there exists a constant
A>0
such that all
n
sufficiently large,

\mathbb{P}\big(\exists v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[(A\log n)^{\eta_{\mathrm{type}}/\zeta_{\mathrm{type}}},\infty):v\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)\leq n^{-C}.

The statement remains true for the Palm-version
\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}
for any
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.

Proof.

We omit the subscript
\mathrm{type}
in the following proof for
\mathrm{type}\in\{\mathrm{ll},\mathrm{hl}\}
.
Let
A=A(C)>0
be sufficiently large. Using the upper bound on
|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|
in
Proposition

6.1
,
and the lower bound on
|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|
from
Corollary

5.8
, we obtain

(6.25)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\leq(A\log n)^{1/\zeta}\}\cap\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|\geq(1/A)n\}\big)\geq 1-o(n^{-C}).

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(\exists v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[(A\log n)^{\eta/\zeta},\infty):v\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)\leq\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\left(\begin{aligned} &\{\exists v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[(A\log n)^{\eta/\zeta},\infty):v\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\}\\
&\cap\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\leq(A\log n)^{1/\zeta}\}\\
&\cap\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|\geq\tfrac{1}{A}n\}\end{aligned}\right)+o(n^{-C}).

On the intersection of the three events on the right-hand side, there is no linear-sized component in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
that contains all vertices with mark above
(A\log n)^{\eta/\zeta}
, which is exactly the negation of the event in (
5.39
). This has probability at most
o(n^{-C})
by (
5.39
) in
Corollary

5.8
, finishing the proof.
∎

Propositions
6.8
and
6.10
give the respective lower and upper bounds for
Theorem

2.9
.

Proof of
Theorem

2.9
.

We prove
Theorem

2.9
when
\max(\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}},\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}})>\max(\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}},0)
. The lower bound follows from (
6.15
) in
Proposition

6.8
, since the prerequisite (
6.14
) is satisfied by
Claim

6.9
. For the upper bound we verify the three prerequisites needed in Proposition
6.10
. Prerequisite (
6.20
) about the second-largest component is proven in Proposition
6.1
; prerequisite (
6.21
) requiring that the largest component is at least polynomial in size follows from
Corollary

5.8
; prerequisite (
6.22
) is proven in
Claim

6.11
. The upper bound in
Theorem

2.9
then follows from (
6.23
).
∎

6.3.
Lower tail of Large Deviations

We are ready to combine everything and give the proof of the lower tail of large deviations for the giant in (
2.21
).
We cite the lower bound from
[
48
]
. We write
{\mathcal{Z}}=\{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}},\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{short}}\}
, with maximum value
\zeta_{\star}
, and
\mathfrak{m}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}
the multiplicity of the maximum, see (
2.16
).

Theorem 6.12

(Lower bound for the lower tail
[
48
, Theorem 2.7]
)
.

Consider a supercritical KSRG on a PPP satisfying Assumption
2.2
or consider supercritical long-range percolation on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
. There exists a constant
A>0
such that for all
\rho>0
, and
n
sufficiently large,

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|<\rho n\big)\geq\exp\big(-\tfrac{1}{A\rho}n^{\zeta_{\star}}(\log n)^{\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{Z}}-1}\big).

Proof of
Theorem

2.7
.

The lower bound follows from
Theorem

6.12
, since the above theorem is valid for all
\rho>0
(but is far from being sharp when
\rho\geq\theta
). When
\max(\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}},\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}})>0
we argue as follows for the upper bound: since we verified the three prerequisites needed in Proposition
6.10
already in the proof of
Theorem

2.9
above, the second conclusion of Proposition
6.10
gives that

|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|\big/n\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\theta.

Hence, we may choose any
\widetilde{\rho}\in(\rho,\theta)
, and it follows that
\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|\geq\widetilde{\rho}n\}
whp.
All highest-mark vertices are in the giant whp by
Claim

6.11
. This proves the requirement (
5.3
) in Proposition
5.1
, i.e.,
\mathbb{P}(A_{\mathrm{giant}}^{\mathrm{type}}(\widetilde{\rho}))\to 1
holds.
Applying
Proposition

5.1
for
\varepsilon>0
such that
\rho=(1-\varepsilon)\widetilde{\rho}
proves the upper bound when
\max(\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}},\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}})>0
.

When
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}>0
,
|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|
contains all vertices of mark above some polylog of
n
by
[
48
, Proposition 5.12]
, and an LLN for
|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|
also holds, see
[
48
, Corollary 2.3]
. So, the same reasoning as above can be applied also when
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}>0
. Since
\zeta_{\mathrm{long}}=\max(\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}})
by
Claim

2.3
, this concludes the proof of
Theorem

2.7
.
∎

7.
Upper tail of large deviations

In this section we deal with the upper tail, that is, with the event of the giant being too large, and prove
Theorem

2.4
and
Theorem

2.5
following the roadmap outlined in Section
3.1
.
We first show a Law of Large Numbers (LLN) for components of size
\ell
that applies to both homogeneous models, i.e.,
\tau=\infty
, and degree-inhomogeneous models, i.e.,
\tau<\infty
. Afterwards, we obtain bounds on the convergence rate of this LLN. We prove that the concentration is exponentially fast when
\tau=\infty
, and show that
Theorem

2.4
follows. Then we analyze the effect of the presence of high-mark vertices (when
\tau<\infty
) to prove
Theorem

2.5
.

7.1.
Concentration of size-
\ell
components

We introduce notation for the probability of the component of the origin being of size
\ell
, and for the total number of size-
\ell
components in the graph induced by vertices with mark at most
\overline{w}_{n}
:

(7.1)

\theta_{\ell}:=\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}(0)|=\ell\big),\quad S_{n,\ell}(\overline{w}_{n}):=\big|\{\text{comp. }{\mathcal{C}}\text{ in }{\mathcal{G}}_{n}[1,\overline{w}_{n}):|{\mathcal{C}}|=\ell\}\big|,\quad S_{n,\ell}:=S_{n,\ell}(\infty).

Clearly
\sum_{1\leq\ell<\infty}\theta_{\ell}=\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}(|{\mathcal{C}}(0)|<\infty)=1-\theta
.
In models with all marks equal
1
(e.g. long-range percolation) it holds
S_{\ell,n}(\overline{w}_{n})=S_{\ell,n}
for any
\overline{w}_{n}>1
.

Lemma 7.1

(LLN for size-
\ell
components)
.

Consider a KSRG on a PPP or on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
satisfying Assumption
2.2
.
Let
\underline{w}_{n}=\omega(n^{1/(\tau-1)})
be a sequence. Then, for every fixed
\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

(7.2)

\frac{\ell S_{n,\ell}(\underline{w}_{n})}{n}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\theta_{\ell},\qquad\text{as }n\to\infty.

Proof.

We give the proof for KSRGs on a PPP with
\tau<\infty
, i.e., non-constant marks. The proof is analogous when
\tau=\infty
or when vertex locations are given by
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
.
By the intensity of
{\mathcal{V}}_{n}
in (
4.5
),
\Lambda_{n}
contains no vertices of mark at least
\underline{w}_{n}
whp. If such vertices are absent, then
S_{n,\ell}(\underline{w}_{n})=S_{n,\ell}(\infty)
. Therefore, for any
\psi>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|\ell S_{n,\ell}(\underline{w}_{n})/n-\theta_{\ell}|>\psi\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\{|\ell S_{n,\ell}(\infty)/n-\theta_{\ell}|>\psi\}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[\underline{w}_{n},\infty)=\emptyset\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{V}}_{n}[\underline{w}_{n},\infty)\neq\emptyset\big)

(7.3)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(|\ell S_{n,\ell}(\infty)/n-\theta_{\ell}|>\psi\big)+o(1).

We use local convergence to show that the probability on the right-hand side vanishes. We refer to
[
72
]
for an elaborate discussion on local convergence and give a short definition.

A rooted graph is a couple
(G,o)
of a graph
G
and some, possibly random, distinguished vertex
o
of
G
, which we call the root of
G
. A finite rooted graph
(G,o)
is said to be uniformly rooted, if
o
is chosen uniformly at random among the vertices of
G
. Let
{\mathcal{G}}_{\star}
be the space of all rooted locally finite graphs. Let
B_{G}(v,r)
denote the induced subgraph of
G
on all vertices that are at graph distance at most
r
from a vertex
v
. For
(G_{1},o_{1}),(G_{2},o_{2})\in{\mathcal{G}}_{\star}
, we define
R(G_{1},G_{2})
to be the largest value
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
B_{G_{1}}(o_{1},r)
is isomorphic to
B_{G_{2}}(o_{2},r)
: there exists a bijection
\phi:V(B_{G_{1}}(o_{1},r))\mapsto V(B_{G_{2}}(o_{2},r)
such that
\phi(o_{1})=o_{2}
and
\{u,v\}
is an edge in
B_{G_{1}}(o_{1},r)
if and only if
\{\phi(u),\phi(v)\}
is an edge in
B_{G_{2}}(o_{1},r)
. We set
d((G_{1},o_{1}),(G_{2},o_{2})):=1/(1+R(G_{1},o_{1}),(G_{2},o_{2})))
for the distance between two rooted graphs.
A sequence of uniformly rooted graphs
(G_{n},o_{n})_{n\geq 1}
converges locally in probability towards
(G_{\infty},o)
having law
\mu
, if for every bounded and continuous function
h:{\mathcal{G}}_{\star}\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}
,

(7.4)

\mathbb{E}\big[h(G_{n},o_{n})\mid G_{n}\big]\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[h(G_{\infty},o)],\qquad\text{as }n\to\infty,

where the expectation on the left-hand side is only with respect to the uniform root. (The limit object
(G_{\infty},o)
may be random and infinite, we here omit this discussion).

Considering KSRGs in Definition
2.1
, the sequence
({\mathcal{G}}_{n})_{n\geq 1}
of finite KSRGs converges locally in probability to the infinite graph
{\mathcal{G}}_{\infty}
conditioned to contain a vertex at the origin, the root, see
[
73
]
. We let
h(G_{n},o_{n})=\mathds{1}_{\{|{\mathcal{C}}(o_{n})|=\ell\}}
be the indicator that the component of
o_{n}
has size
\ell
.
Then,
h
is bounded, and continuous on the Polish space
({\mathcal{G}}_{\star},d)
: it only depends on the induced subgraph up to distance
\ell+1
from the root, so
h(G_{1},o_{1})=h(G_{2},o_{2})
for any two rooted graphs
(G_{1},o_{1}),(G_{2},o_{2})
within distance at most
1/(\ell+2)
. So (
7.4
) applies and the right-hand side there equals
\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}(|{\mathcal{C}}(0)|=\ell)=\theta_{\ell}
. Instead of explicitly writing out the left-hand side, we first notice that
\sum_{o_{n}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}}h(G_{n},o_{n})=\ell S_{n,\ell}(\infty)
.
Therefore,

(7.5)

\frac{\ell S_{n,\ell}(\infty)}{n}=\frac{|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}|}{n}\frac{1}{|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}|}\sum_{o_{n}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}}h(G_{n},o_{n})=\frac{|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}|}{n}\cdot\mathbb{E}[h(G_{n},o_{n})\mid G_{n}]\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}1\cdot\theta_{\ell},

where we used that the ratio
|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}|/n
tends to one in probability. Hence, the probability on the right-hand side in (
7.3
) tends to zero as
n
tends to infinity. This finishes the proof for PPP vertex sets. When the locations are given by
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
, or
\tau=\infty
, the result follows analogously.
∎

7.2.
Homogeneous models

We prove a bound on the convergence rate in
Lemma

7.1
using a boxing argument.

Lemma 7.2

(Concentration for size-
\ell
components)
.

Consider long-range percolation with
\alpha>1
or Bernoulli bond percolation on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
. For all fixed
\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
\psi>0
, there exists a constant
\varepsilon_{\ell,\psi}>0
such that for all
n\geq 1
,

(7.6)

\mathbb{P}\Big(\big|S_{n,\ell}(\infty)/(n\theta_{\ell}/\ell)-1\big|\leq\psi\Big)\geq 1-\exp\big(-\varepsilon_{\ell,\psi}n\big).

Proof.

Let
k
be a large constant, and w.l.o.g. assume
that
\Lambda_{n}
can be partitioned into
(n/k)
volume-
k
subboxes. Let
S_{k,\ell}^{\scriptscriptstyle(j)}
denote the number of size-
\ell
components in the induced subgraph inside the
j
th box. Then, by
Lemma

7.1
, for a small constant
\psi_{1}>0
, independently across boxes it holds that

\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{A}}_{\ell}(j,k)):=\mathbb{P}\Big(|S_{k,\ell}^{\scriptscriptstyle(j)}/(k\theta_{\ell}/\ell)-1|\leq\psi_{1}\Big)\geq 1-\psi_{1}/2.

The number of boxes for which this event holds dominates a binomial random variable with parameters
n/k
and
1-\psi_{1}/2
. So if we set

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}(k):=\bigg\{\Big|\sum_{j\in[n/k]}\mathds{1}_{\{{\mathcal{A}}_{\ell}(j,k)\}}-n/k\Big|\leq\psi_{1}n/k\bigg\},

then by a Chernoff bound, there exists a constant
c=c(\psi_{1})>0
such that

(7.7)

\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}(k)\big)\leq\exp\big(-c(n/k)\big).

On
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}(k)
, since
(n/k)k\theta_{\ell}/\ell=n\theta_{\ell}/\ell
, the number of size-
\ell
components of in the union of the induced subgraphs satisfies

(7.8)

(1-\psi_{1})^{2}n\theta_{\ell}/\ell\leq\sum_{j\in[n/k]}S_{k,\ell}^{\scriptscriptstyle(j)}\leq(1+\psi_{1})^{2}n\theta_{\ell}/\ell\qquad\mbox{on}\ {\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}(k).

We now look at edges across subboxes. Let
{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathrm{cross}}(k)
denote the set of vertices having an edge to a vertex in a different subbox.
Each vertex in
{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathrm{cross}}(k)
changes the number of size-
\ell
components by at most
1
: indeed, this number increases by one when its crossing edge(s) form a new component of size-
\ell
, and it decreases by one if without crossing edges its size was
\ell
.
Therefore,

(7.9)

\Big|S_{n,\ell}(\infty)-\sum_{j\in[n/k]}S_{k,\ell}^{\scriptscriptstyle(j)}\Big|\leq|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathrm{cross}}(k)|.

Hence, for sufficiently small
\psi_{1}
depending on
\psi
and
\ell
, on the event on
\{|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathrm{cross}}(k)|<\psi_{1}n\big\}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}(k)
,

(1-\psi)n\theta_{\ell}/\ell\leq(1-\psi_{1})^{2}n\theta_{\ell}/\ell-\psi_{1}n\leq S_{n,\ell}(\infty)\leq(1+\psi_{1})^{2}n\theta_{\ell}/\ell+\psi_{1}n\leq(1+\psi)n\theta_{\ell}/\ell.

Combining this with (
7.7
), the exponential decay in (
7.6
) holds if

(7.10)

\forall\psi_{1}>0:\exists k,n_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}},\varepsilon>0\quad\mbox{s.t.}\quad\forall n\geq n_{0}:\qquad\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathrm{cross}}(k)|\geq\psi_{1}n\big)\leq\exp\big(-\varepsilon n\big).

For Bernoulli bond percolation on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
, the number of vertices with a crossing edge in each subbox is deterministically at most
O(k^{(d-1)/d})
as
k\to\infty
, so the total number of vertices with a crossing edge across the
(n/k)
many subboxes is
nO(k^{-1/d})
. So, given
\psi_{1}
, we choose
k
large enough so that
O(k^{-1/d})<\psi_{1}
, and then (
7.10
) holds (with error probability
0
).

Consider long-range percolation on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
with
\alpha>1
.
Let
{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathrm{surf}}(t)
be the set of vertices within distance
t
from the union of the boundaries of all subboxes
j\leq n/k
; and let
{\mathcal{E}}_{n}^{\mathrm{long}}(t)
denote the edges of length at least
t
inside
\Lambda_{n}
. Clearly,
|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathrm{surf}}(t)|\leq(n/k)2^{d}k^{(d-1)/d}(2t+1)
.
For any
\psi_{1}>0
, fix
\psi_{2}>0
such that
|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathrm{surf}}(\psi_{2}k^{1/d})|\leq\psi_{1}n/2
holds deterministically for all
k
sufficiently large (simultaneously).
Then, since each edge has two endpoints,

|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathrm{cross}}(k)|\leq|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathrm{surf}}(\psi_{2}k^{1/d})|+2|{\mathcal{E}}_{n}^{\mathrm{long}}(\psi_{2}k^{1/d})|\leq\psi_{1}n/2+2|{\mathcal{E}}_{n}^{\mathrm{long}}(\psi_{2}k^{1/d})|.

Therefore,

(7.11)

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathrm{cross}}(k)|\geq\psi_{1}n\big)\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(2\big|{\mathcal{E}}_{n}^{\mathrm{long}}(\psi_{2}k^{1/d})\big|\geq\psi_{1}n/2\Big).

By a standard isoperimetric inequality, the total number of possible edges inside
\Lambda_{n}
with length in the interval
[r_{i,k},r_{i+1,k}):=[2^{i}\psi_{2}k^{1/d},2^{i+1}\psi_{2}k^{1/d})
is at most
n\cdot O(r_{i,k}^{d})
as
i\to\infty
.
Each such edge is present independently with probability at most
\beta^{\alpha}r_{i,k}^{-\alpha d}
by the connection probability (
4.6
). Hence, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for
k
sufficiently large

\displaystyle\big|{\mathcal{E}}_{n}^{\mathrm{long}}(\psi_{2}k^{1/d})\big|

\displaystyle\preccurlyeq\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\mathrm{Bin}\big(C\psi_{2}^{d}n2^{id}k,\beta^{\alpha}\psi_{2}^{-\alpha d}2^{-i\alpha d}k^{-\alpha}\big),

where all binomial random variables in the sum are independent of each other.
Thus,

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathrm{cross}}(k)|\geq\psi_{1}n\big)\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg(2\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\mathrm{Bin}\big(C\psi_{2}^{d}n2^{id}k,\beta^{\alpha}\psi_{2}^{-\alpha d}(2^{id}k)^{-\alpha}\big)\geq\psi_{1}n/2\bigg).

First we apply
x\mapsto\exp(x/2)
to both sides of the inequality inside the probability on the right-hand side, then we use Markov’s inequality and independence of the random variables:

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathrm{cross}}(k)|\geq\psi_{1}n\big)\leq{\mathrm{e}}^{-\psi_{1}n/4}\prod_{i=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp\Big(\mathrm{Bin}\big(C\psi_{2}^{d}n2^{id}k,\beta^{\alpha}\psi_{2}^{-\alpha d}(2^{id}k)^{-\alpha}\big)\Big)\Big].

Each factor corresponds to the moment-generating function (MGF) of a binomial random variable evaluated at 1. The MGF of
\mathrm{Bin}(n,p)
at 1 equals
(1+p({\mathrm{e}}-1))^{n}\leq\exp\big(({\mathrm{e}}-1)np\big)
. Applying this bound to each of the factors yields

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\mathrm{cross}}(k)|\geq\psi_{1}n\big)\leq\exp\bigg(-\psi_{1}n/4+({\mathrm{e}}-1)C\beta^{\alpha}\psi_{2}^{d(1-\alpha)}nk^{1-\alpha}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}2^{id(1-\alpha)}\bigg).

Since
\alpha>1
by assumption, the sum converges. When
k
is sufficiently large, the right-hand side is at most
\exp(-\psi_{1}n/5)
. This proves (
7.10
) for long-range percolation and hence (
7.6
).
∎

We are ready to prove
Theorem

2.4
.

Proof of
Theorem

2.4
.

For the lower bound, we observe that
|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|=n
when all nearest neighbor edges are present. Its probability decays as
\exp(-\Theta(n))
.
For the upper bound, we argue as follows. We fix
\rho\in(\theta,1)
, and a small
\psi<1-(1-\rho)/(1-\theta)
. Since
\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\theta_{\ell}=1-\theta
, this gives us a constant
\ell^{\star}=\ell^{\star}(\rho,\psi)
that satisfies
(1-\psi)\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell^{\ast}}\theta_{\ell}>1-\rho
. We shall shortly show that

(7.12)

\{{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}>\rho n\}\subseteq\big\{\exists\ell\leq\ell^{\star}+1:S_{n,\ell}(\infty)\leq(1-\psi)n\theta_{\ell}/\ell\big\}=:{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}.

If (
7.12
) holds, then
Lemma

7.2
and a union bound over the finite set
\ell\leq\ell^{\ast}+1
gives that
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}
occurs with probability exponentially small in
n
, finishing the proof of the upper bound. To prove (
7.12
), we show that
\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\subseteq\{{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\leq\rho n\}
.
Since we excluded the case in LRP that nearest neighbor edges are present with probability one,
\theta_{\ell}=\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}(0)|=\ell\big)>0
for all
\ell\geq 1
. So, for all
n
sufficiently large, on
\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}
, there is at least one component of size
\ell^{\star}+1
; i.e.,
|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}}|\geq\ell^{\ast}+1
. Hence, vertices in components of size
\ell\leq\ell^{\star}
are not in the giant. Thus,

n-|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|=|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\setminus{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})|\geq\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell^{\ast}}\ell S_{n,\ell}(\infty)\geq\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell^{\ast}}(1-\psi)n\theta_{\ell}>n(1-\rho).

Rearranging yields that
|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}}|<\rho n
on
\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}
, finishing the proof.
∎

7.3.
Inhomogeneous models

Here we start again with the convergence rate of the number of size-
\ell
components. Since the degree distribution is heavy-tailed, this is more involved than Lemma
7.2
.
We write
\mathrm{deg}_{v}
for the degree of vertex
v
in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
, and
\mathrm{deg}_{v}[a,b)
for the number of neighbors of
v
in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
with mark in
[a,b)
.

Claim 7.3

(Total degree from high-mark vertices)
.

Consider a KSRG on a PPP satisfying Assumption
2.2
with
\tau<\infty
. For any constants
\psi,C>0
, there exists
\phi_{0}>0
such that for all
n
sufficiently large and
\phi\in(0,\phi_{0})
, writing
\underline{w}_{n}=n^{(1+\psi)/(\tau-1)}
,

(7.13)

\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sum_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[\underline{w}_{n},\phi n)}\mathrm{deg}_{v}[1,\phi n)\geq\psi n\Bigg)\leq n^{-C}.

Claim 7.4

(High-degree vertices)
.

Consider a KSRG on a PPP satisfying Assumption
2.2
with
\tau<\infty
. For any constants
\ell,C>0
, there exist constants
n_{0}
,
C_{1}
such that for all
n\geq n_{0}
, and any
\underline{w}_{n}>C_{1}\log n
,

\mathbb{P}\big(\exists v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[\underline{w}_{n},\infty):\mathrm{deg}_{v}[1,\underline{w}_{n})<\ell\big)\leq n^{-C}.

Claim 7.5

(Crossing edges)
.

Consider a KSRG on a PPP satisfying Assumption
2.2
with
\tau<\infty
. Let
\psi>0
be a constant such that
(1+\psi)/(\tau-1)<1
. Then, for any constant
C>0
, there exists
n_{0}
such that for all
n\geq n_{0}
and any
\underline{w}_{n}\leq n^{(1+\psi)/(\tau-1)}
,

(7.14)

\mathbb{P}\big(|\{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[1,\underline{w}_{n}):v\sim\Lambda_{n}^{\mathsf{c}}\times[1,\underline{w}_{n})\}|\geq\psi n\big)\leq n^{-C}.

The proofs of the three claims are based on concentration inequalities for Poisson/binomial random variables and postponed to Appendix
C.1
. Their combination gives a
polynomial
convergence rate for the size-
\ell
components in
Lemma

7.1
