, which is in stark contrast to the exponential convergence rate for degree-homogeneous models in
Lemma

7.2
.

Lemma 7.6

(Concentration for size-
\ell
components)
.

Consider a KSRG on a PPP satisfying Assumption
2.2
with
\tau<\infty
. For all constants
\ell,\psi,C>0
, there exist constants
\phi_{0}>0
and
n_{0}
such that for all
n\geq n_{0}
and all
\phi\in(0,\phi_{0})
,

(7.15)

\mathbb{P}\big(\big|S_{n,\ell}(\phi n)/n-\theta_{\ell}/\ell\big|>\psi\big)\leq n^{-C}.

Proof.

We first reduce the upper mark threshold
\overline{w}_{n}:=\phi n
in the event in (
7.15
) to a smaller value
\underline{w}_{n}=n^{(1+\psi)/(\tau-1)}
(we assume w.l.o.g. that
(1+\psi)/(\tau-1)<1
). We use that each vertex with degree at least
\ell
is in a component of size at least
\ell+1
, and distinguish the case that each vertex of mark
\underline{w}_{n}
is in a component of size larger than
\ell
, i.e.,

(7.16)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\big|S_{n,\ell}(\overline{w}_{n})/n-\theta_{\ell}/\ell\big|>\psi\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg(\begin{aligned} &\{|S_{n,\ell}(\overline{w}_{n})/n-\theta_{\ell}/\ell|>\psi\}\\
&\cap\{\forall v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[\underline{w}_{n},\overline{w}_{n}):|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(v)[1,\overline{w}_{n})|>\ell\}\end{aligned}\bigg)

(7.17)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\mathbb{P}\big(\exists v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[\underline{w}_{n},\overline{w}_{n}):\mathrm{deg}_{v}[1,\overline{w}_{n})\leq\ell\big).

Now we ‘switch’
\overline{w}_{n}
to
\underline{w}_{n}
in
S_{n,\ell}(\cdot)
on the right-hand side. Let
T_{n,\ell}
denote the number of size-
\ell
components in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}[1,\underline{w}_{n})
that have an edge towards a vertex with mark in
[\underline{w}_{n},\overline{w}_{n})
.
The events on the right-hand side of (
7.16
) imply that the size-
\ell
components of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}[1,\overline{w}_{n})
do not contain any vertices with mark at least
\overline{w}_{n}
.
So, on those events,
S_{n,\ell}(\overline{w}_{n})=S_{n,\ell}(\underline{w}_{n})-T_{n,\ell}
. Distinguishing also whether
T_{n,\ell}/n\leq\psi/2
or not yields

(7.18)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\big|S_{n,\ell}(\overline{w}_{n})/n-\theta_{\ell}/\ell\big|>\psi\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg(\begin{aligned} &\{|S_{n,\ell}(\underline{w}_{n})/n-T_{n,\ell}/n-\theta_{\ell}/\ell|>\psi\}\cap\{T_{n,\ell}/n\leq\psi/2\}\\
&\cap\{\forall v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[\underline{w}_{n},\overline{w}_{n}):|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(v)[1,\overline{w}_{n})|>\ell\}\end{aligned}\bigg)

(7.19)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\mathbb{P}\big(T_{n,\ell}/n\geq\psi/2\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(\exists v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[\underline{w}_{n},\overline{w}_{n}):\mathrm{deg}_{v}[1,\overline{w}_{n})\leq\ell\big).

The first two events in (
7.18
) imply that
\{|S_{n,\ell}(\underline{w}_{n})/n-\theta_{\ell}/\ell|>\psi/2\}
. Moreover,
T_{n,\ell}
is at most the total number of edges between
{\mathcal{V}}[\underline{w}_{n},\overline{w}_{n})
and
{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[1,\underline{w}_{n})
, which is at most the total degree of vertices in
{\mathcal{V}}[\underline{w}_{n},\overline{w}_{n})
. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\big|S_{n,\ell}(\overline{w}_{n})/n-\theta_{\ell}/\ell\big|>\psi\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(|S_{n,\ell}(\underline{w}_{n})/n-\theta_{\ell}/\ell|>\psi/2\big)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sum_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[\underline{w}_{n},\overline{w}_{n})}\mathrm{deg}_{v}[1,\overline{w}_{n})\geq n\psi/2\bigg)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\big(\exists v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[\underline{w}_{n},\overline{w}_{n}):\mathrm{deg}_{v}[1,\overline{w}_{n})\leq\ell\big).

By Claim
7.3
, the second term can be made at most
n^{-2C}
if
\phi
in the definition of
\overline{w}_{n}=\phi n
is sufficiently small. Since
\underline{w}_{n}=n^{(1+\psi)/(\tau-1)}
, the third term is also at most
n^{-2C}
by Claim
7.4
. So,

(7.20)

\mathbb{P}\big(\big|S_{n,\ell}(\overline{w}_{n})/n-\theta_{\ell}/\ell\big|>\psi\big)\leq\mathbb{P}\big(|S_{n,\ell}(\underline{w}_{n})/n-\theta_{\ell}/\ell|>\psi/2\big)+o\big(n^{-C}\big).

It remains to bound the first term on the right-hand side with
\underline{w}_{n}=n^{(1+\psi)/(\tau-1)}
.
Let
A
be a sufficiently large constant with
(A\log n)^{1/d}\in{\mathbb{N}}
and partition
\Lambda_{n}
into boxes
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathcal{Q}}_{A\log n}
of volume
k_{n}:=n/(A\log n)
. Let
S_{k_{n},\ell}^{\scriptscriptstyle(j)}(\underline{w}_{n})
denote the number of components of size exactly
\ell
in the
j
th box, and
{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(j)}_{k_{n}}
the set of vertices in the
j
th box.

(7.21)

{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(j)}:=\{|S_{k_{n},\ell}^{\scriptscriptstyle(j)}(\underline{w}_{n})/k_{n}-\theta_{\ell}/\ell|\leq\psi/12\},

and otherwise we call it bad. The probability of being good tends to
1
with
k_{n}
by
Lemma

7.1
, and so it is at least
1-\psi/32
for all
n
sufficiently large.
Let
X:=\sum_{j\in[k_{n}]}\mathds{1}_{\{{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(j)}\}}

be the number of good boxes. Independence across boxes gives that
X
dominates a binomial random variable with parameters
A\log n
and
1-\psi/32
. So, by a Chernoff bound there exists a constant
c=c(\psi)>0
such that

(7.22)

\mathbb{P}\big(X\geq(1-\psi/16)A\log n\big)\geq 1-\exp\big(-cA\log n\big)=1-n^{-cA}.

Since
k_{n}=n/A\log n
, using concentration for Poisson random variables, for all
n
sufficiently large,

(7.23)

\mathbb{P}\big(\forall j:|{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(j)}[1,\underline{w}_{n})|/k_{n}<4/3\big)\geq 1-(A\log n)\exp\big(-\Omega(n/\log n)\big)\geq 1-n^{-cA}.

Thus, in the following, we will find bounds on
S_{n,\ell}(\underline{w}_{n})
conditional on the event

(7.24)

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{good}}:=\{\forall i:|{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(j)}[1,\underline{w}_{n})|/k_{n}<4/3\}\cap\{X\geq(1-\psi/16)A\log n\}.

We write
S^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{good}}_{n,\ell}
for the sum of
S_{k_{n},\ell}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(j)}}
over
j
for which the
j
th box is good, and similarly
S^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{bad}}_{k_{n},\ell}
for the same sum over bad boxes.
Finally, let
{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{cross}}_{n}[1,\underline{w}_{n})
denote the set of vertices incident to an edge that crosses a box-boundary. Since each edge changes the number of size-
\ell
components by at most
2
, similarly to (
7.9
), it holds that

(7.25)

|S_{n,\ell}(\underline{w}_{n})-(S^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{good}}_{n,\ell}+S^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{bad}}_{n,\ell})|\leq|{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{cross}}_{n}[1,\underline{w}_{n})|.

On the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{good}}
in (
7.24
), the at most
A\log n
good boxes contain together at most
(\theta_{\ell}/\ell+\psi/12)n
components of size
\ell
. There are at most
n(\psi/16)4/3=n\psi/12
vertices in bad boxes. Assuming the worst case that all of them are in size-
\ell
components inside their boxes, we obtain the upper bound
S^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{bad}}_{n,\ell}\leq n\psi/(12\ell)
. So, by a triangle inequality on the left-hand side above,

S_{n,\ell}(\underline{w}_{n})-n\theta_{\ell}/\ell\leq n\psi/12+n\psi/(12\ell)+|{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{cross}}_{n}[1,\underline{w}_{n})|\leq n\psi/6+|{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{cross}}_{n}[1,\underline{w}_{n})|.

Similarly, for a lower bound we may assume that there are no size-
\ell
components in bad boxes. On the event (
7.24
), this still gives

S_{n,\ell}(\underline{w}_{n})-n\theta_{\ell}/\ell\geq n(1-\psi/16)\big(\theta_{\ell}/\ell-\psi/12\big)-|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{cross}}[1,\underline{w}_{n})|\geq-n\psi 7/48-|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{cross}}[1,\underline{w}_{n})|

Recalling the bounds (
7.20
), (
7.22
), and (
7.23
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\big|S_{n,\ell}(\overline{w}_{n})/n-\theta_{\ell}/\ell\big|>\psi\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2n^{-cA}+o(n^{-C})+\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{cross}}_{n,\ell}|/n\geq\psi/3\big).

We bound the probability concerning the crossing edges. By the pigeon-hole principle,
|{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{cross}}_{n,\ell}|\geq n\psi/3
implies that there exists a box that contains at least
(\psi/3)k_{n}
vertices with an outgoing edge towards the complement of the box. Hence, by translation invariance of the model on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{V}}_{n,\ell}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{cross}}|/n\geq\psi/3\big)\leq(A\log n)\mathbb{P}\big(|\{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{k_{n}}[1,\underline{w}_{n}):v\sim\Lambda_{k_{n}}^{\mathsf{c}}\times[1,\underline{w}_{n})\}|\geq(\psi/3)k_{n}\big).

Claim

7.5
is applicable here for
n_{\ref{claim:crossing-edges}}=k_{n}=n/(A\log n)
, since
\underline{w}_{n}=n^{(1+\psi)/(\tau-1)}\leq k_{n}^{(1+\psi^{\prime})/(\tau-1)}
for any
\psi^{\prime}>\psi
. So the right-hand side is smaller than
n^{-cA}
for
n
sufficiently large. We conclude that (
7.15
) follows for
\phi
small, setting
A\geq C/c
such that
(A\log n)^{1/d}\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
∎

We need a last preliminary claim.
Recall
\mathrm{hubs}(\rho)
from (
2.17
). We introduce notation for the functions appearing in the upper and lower bound of
Theorem

2.5
:

(7.26)

h_{\mathrm{up}}:=\lceil\mathrm{hubs}(\rho)\rceil,\qquad h_{\mathrm{lo}}:=\lim_{r\downarrow\rho}\lceil\mathrm{hubs}(r)\rceil=\begin{cases}\lceil\mathrm{hubs}(\rho)\rceil,&\text{if }\mathrm{hubs}(\rho)\notin{\mathbb{N}}\text{ or }p=1,\\
\mathrm{hubs}(\rho)+1,&\text{if }\mathrm{hubs}(\rho)\in{\mathbb{N}}\text{ and }p<1.\end{cases}

Clearly
h_{\mathrm{lo}}\geq h_{\mathrm{up}}\geq\mathrm{hubs}(\rho)
, and for all
\rho\in(\theta,1)
and
p<1
also
h_{\mathrm{lo}}>\mathrm{hubs}(\rho)
strictly.

Claim 7.7

(Truncation of the generating function)
.

Consider a KSRG on a PPP satisfying Assumption
2.2
with
\tau<\infty
. Let
\rho\in(\theta,1)
. There exist constants
\psi>0
and
\ell_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that for all
\ell^{\ast}>\ell_{0}
,

(7.27)

(1-\psi)^{2}\bigg(\theta+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell^{\ast}}\big(1-(1-p)^{h_{\mathrm{lo}}\ell}\big)\theta_{\ell}\bigg)>\rho,

and

(7.28)

(1-\psi)^{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell^{\ast}}\theta_{\ell}(1-p)^{(h_{\mathrm{up}}-1)\ell}>1-\rho+\psi.

We postpone the proof to Appendix
C.2
and proceed to the lower bound of
Theorem

2.5
.

Proposition 7.8

(Lower bound of the upper tail)
.

Consider a KSRG on a PPP satisfying Assumption
2.2
with
\tau<\infty
.
Fix
\rho\in(\theta,1)
.
Assume either supercriticality and
\zeta_{\mathrm{long}}>0
, or assume
\theta=0
. Then there exists
A=A(\rho)>0
such that for all
n\geq 1
,

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|>\rho n\big)\geq(1/A)n^{-(\tau-2)h_{\mathrm{lo}}}.

Proof.

The proof is similar in spirit to the proof of
Theorem

2.4
on page
7.2
, but we also need to deal with high-mark vertices. See Section
3.2
for an outline that we make formal now.
Let
R,\ell^{\star}
be two sufficiently large constants, and let
\phi,\psi>0
be two small constants so that given
\rho\in(\theta,1)
, (
7.27
) holds.
Write
T_{n,\ell}
for the number of components of size
\ell
in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}[1,\phi n)
that connect by an edge to the vertices with mark in
{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[Rn,\infty)
. Define the events

(7.29)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}

\displaystyle:=\big\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,\phi n)|/n\geq\theta(1-\psi)\big\}\cap\big\{\forall\ell\leq\ell^{\ast}:S_{n,\ell}(\phi n)\geq(1-\psi)n\theta_{\ell}/\ell\big\},

(7.30)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hub}}

\displaystyle:=\big\{|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[Rn,\infty)|=h_{\mathrm{lo}}\big\}\cap\big\{\forall v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[Rn,\infty):v\sim{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,\phi n)\big\},

(7.31)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{conn}}

\displaystyle:=\big\{\forall\ell\leq\ell^{\ast}:T_{n,\ell}\geq(1-\psi)\big(1-(1-p)^{h_{\mathrm{lo}}\ell}\big)\big((1-\psi)n\theta_{\ell}/\ell\big)\big\}.

We shall show that

(7.32)

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hub}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{conn}}\subseteq\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|>\rho n\}.

By its definition,
T_{n,\ell}
many clusters of size
\ell
connect to the hubs listed in
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hub}}
, which then all are part of the giant component in the whole graph. Hence, on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hub}}
, the giant
|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,\phi n)|
increases by at least
\ell T_{n,\ell}
when fully revealing the graph.
By the bounds on
T_{n,\ell}
and the initial size
(1-\psi)\theta n
of the giant inside the events, and (
7.27
) in
Claim

7.7
, we obtain that

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|/n

\displaystyle\geq\theta(1-\psi)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell^{\ast}}\ell T_{n,\ell}\geq\theta(1-\psi)+(1-\psi)^{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell^{\ast}}\big(1-(1-p)^{h_{\mathrm{lo}}\ell}\big)\theta_{\ell}

\displaystyle\geq(1-\psi)^{2}\bigg(\theta+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell^{\ast}}\big(1-(1-p)^{h_{\mathrm{lo}}\ell}\big)\theta_{\ell}\bigg)>\rho,

so
(
7.32
) holds. We bound from below the probability of the intersection using the law of total probability:

(7.33)

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{conn}}\big)=\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}\big)\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{conn}}\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}\big).

To study the first factor, we use the law of total probability again and then also
\mathbb{P}(A\cap B)\geq\mathbb{P}(A)-\mathbb{P}(\neg B)
(see (
7.29
)-(
7.30
)):

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}\big)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\Big(\forall v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[Rn,\infty):v\sim{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,\phi n)\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\cap\big\{|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[Rn,\infty)|=h_{\mathrm{lo}}\big\}\Big)

(7.34)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[Rn,\infty)|=h_{\mathrm{lo}}\big)-\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\big)\Big).

For the first term in the row (
7.34
) we use that
|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[Rn,\infty)|\sim\mathrm{Poi}(n(Rn)^{-(\tau-1)})
by the intensity in (
4.5
). Note here that the parameter tends to
0
since
\tau>2
. Therefore, since
h_{\mathrm{lo}}
is a
\rho
-dependent constant that does not depend on
n
,

(7.35)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[Rn,\infty)|=h_{\mathrm{lo}}\big)

\displaystyle=\exp\big(\!-\!R^{-(\tau-1)}n^{-(\tau-2)}\big)\frac{\big(R^{-(\tau-1)}n^{-(\tau-2)}\big)^{h_{\mathrm{lo}}}}{h_{\mathrm{lo}}!}\geq c_{R,\rho}n^{-(\tau-2)h_{\mathrm{lo}}}

for some constant
c_{R,\rho}>0
. We now bound
\mathbb{P}(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}})
in (
7.34
).

(7.36)

\mathbb{P}(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}})\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\exists\ell\leq\ell^{\ast}:\ell S_{n,\ell}(\phi n)/n\leq(1-\psi)\theta_{\ell}\Big)+\mathbb{P}(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,\phi n)|/n<\theta(1-\psi)).

Lemma
7.6
and a union bound over the
\ell^{\star}
many
\ell
shows that the first term is at most
n^{-C}
for arbitrary large
C
when
\phi<\phi_{0}(\psi)
. For the second term, there are two cases. Either
\theta=0
and then this probability is
0
; or,
\theta
is non-zero. In the latter case, when additionally
\zeta_{\mathrm{long}}>0
, our result for the
lower
tail of large deviations in
Theorem

2.7
guarantees that this event has stretched exponentially decaying probability in
n
. This is where we use that the model must be supercritical with
\zeta_{\mathrm{long}}>0
when
6
6

6

Inspection of the proof of
Theorem

2.7
shows that the sprinkling technique used in Section
4
needs supercriticality. The assumption
\zeta_{\mathrm{long}}>0
is the technical condition of
Theorem

2.7
that guarantees enough long edges.

\theta>0
.
So, under these conditions, we know that
\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\big)
is of smaller order than the lower bound in (
7.35
) on the first term in (
7.34
).

We now analyze the conditional probability on the line above (
7.34
). Take
R>\sqrt{d}/\beta
. Then a vertex with mark at least
Rn
connects by an edge to any other vertex in
\Lambda_{n}
with probability
p
by (
4.6
). The giant
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}}[1,\phi n)
has size at least
(1-\psi)\theta n
on the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}
in (
7.29
) in the conditioning. So, by a union bound, the (conditional) probability that all the
h_{\mathrm{lo}}
many vertices of mark at least
Rn
connect by an edge to
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}}[1,\phi n)
is at least
1-h_{\mathrm{lo}}(1-p)^{\Theta(n)}
, which tends to
1
as
n\to\infty
. Combining these with (
7.34
)–(
7.35
) gives in (
7.33
)

(7.37)

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}\big)\geq c_{R,\rho}n^{-(\tau-2)h_{\mathrm{lo}}}.

We show that the other term in (
7.33
),
\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{conn}}\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}\big)
tends to
1

as
n\to\infty
. Recall these events from (
7.29
)–(
7.31
) and that under the conditioning,
T_{n,\ell}
denotes the number of size-
\ell
components of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}[1,\phi n)
that connect to one of the
h_{\mathrm{lo}}
many vertices of mark at least
Rn
(the hubs).
As before, each vertex connects independently to these hubs with probability
p
. Further, the vertex set counted in
T_{n,\ell}
is disjoint of the vertices in
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}}[1,\phi n)
so their connection to the hubs is independent of whether the vertices in
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}}[1,\phi n)
connect to the hubs, which is an event inside the conditioning
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}
. So, under the conditioning,
each component of size
\ell
in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}[1,\phi n)
connects to one of the
h_{\mathrm{lo}}
hubs with probability
1-(1-p)^{h_{\mathrm{lo}}\ell}
independently across size-
\ell
components and across
\ell<\ell^{\star}
. Hence, given the number of size-
\ell
components in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}[1,\psi n)
,
(S_{n,\ell}(\phi n))_{\ell\leq\ell^{\star}}
, the variables
T_{n,\ell}
are binomially distributed with parameters
S_{n,\ell}(\phi n)
and
(1-(1-p)^{h_{\mathrm{lo}}\ell})
. Further, under the conditioning in
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}
, each
S_{n,\ell}(\phi n)
is at least
(1-\psi)n\theta_{\ell}/\ell
, so
T_{n,\ell}
dominates a binomial random variable with parameters
(1-\psi)n\theta_{\ell}/\ell
and
(1-(1-p)^{h_{\mathrm{lo}}\ell})
for each
\ell\leq\ell^{\star}
.
A union bound over
\ell\leq\ell^{\star}
and then a Chernoff bound for each
\ell
give that

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{conn}}\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}\big)\to 1
as
n\to\infty
. Using this in (
7.33
) and (
7.37
) gives in (
7.32
) that for some constant
c_{R,\rho}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}}>\rho n)\geq\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{conn}}\big)

\displaystyle\geq c_{R,\rho}n^{-(\tau-2)h_{\mathrm{lo}}}.\qed

We proceed to the upper bound of
Theorem

2.5
.

Proposition 7.9

(Upper bound of the upper tail)
.

Consider a KSRG satisfying Assumption
2.2
on a PPP with
\tau<\infty
.
Fix
\rho\in(\theta,1)
. There exists
A=A(\rho)>0
such that for all
n\geq 1
,

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|>\rho n\big)\leq An^{-(\tau-2)h_{\mathrm{up}}}.

Proof.

Given
\rho
, fix
\phi,\psi>0
sufficiently small and
\ell^{\star}
sufficiently large so that (
7.28
) holds for
\psi
and
\ell^{\star}
.
We write
\overline{T}_{n,\ell}
for the number of components of size
\ell
in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}[1,\phi n)
that do
not
connect by an edge to the vertices with mark at least
\phi n
, and define

(7.38)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}

\displaystyle:=\big\{|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}|/n\leq 1+\psi\}\cap\big\{\forall\ell\leq\ell^{\ast}+1:S_{n,\ell}(\phi n)\geq(1-\psi)n\theta_{\ell}/\ell\big\},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}

\displaystyle:=\big\{|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[\phi n,\infty)|\leq h_{\mathrm{up}}-1\big\},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{conn}}

\displaystyle:=\big\{\forall\ell\in[\ell^{\ast}]:\overline{T}_{n,\ell}>(1-\psi)(1-p)^{(h_{\mathrm{up}}-1)\ell}\big((1-\psi)n\theta_{\ell}/\ell\big)\big\}.

We show in the next paragraph that

(7.39)

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{conn}}\subseteq\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}}|\leq\rho n\}.

To show this, we bound the size of the complement of the giant on the intersection of the three events. Since
S_{n,\ell^{\ast}+1}(\phi n)\!\geq\!1
for all sufficiently large
n
, the largest component has size at least
\ell^{\ast}
. Hence, vertices in components of size
\ell\leq\ell^{\star}
are not in the giant of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}[1,\psi n)
. Combining the bounds in the events with
Claim

7.7
, we obtain for sufficiently small
\psi
that

1-|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|/n\geq|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|/n-\psi\geq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell^{\ast}}\ell\overline{T}_{n,\ell}-\psi\geq(1-\psi)^{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell^{\ast}}\theta_{\ell}(1-p)^{(h_{\mathrm{up}}-1)\ell}-\psi>1-\rho.

Rearranging shows (
7.39
), which implies that
\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|>\rho n\}\subseteq(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}})\cup(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}})\cup(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{conn}})
.
Thus, it follows that

(7.40)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|>\rho n\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{conn}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{conn}}\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}\big).

By
Lemma

7.6
and the concentration of Poisson random variables in
Lemma

A.1
,
\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\big)=o(n^{-C})
for any
C>0
if
\phi=\phi(\psi)
is sufficiently small. For
\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}\big)
, we use similarly to (
7.35
) that the number of hubs is Poisson. So, there exists a constant
C_{\phi,\rho}>0
such that for all
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}\big)=\mathbb{P}\big(\mathrm{Poi}\big(n(\phi n)^{-(\tau-1)}\big)\geq h_{\mathrm{up}}\big)

\displaystyle=\exp\big(-\phi^{-(\tau-1)}n^{-(\tau-2)}\big)\sum_{j=h_{\mathrm{up}}}^{\infty}\frac{\phi^{-j(\tau-1)}n^{-j(\tau-2)}}{j!}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\phi,\rho}n^{-(\tau-2)h_{\mathrm{up}}}.

For the conditional probability in (
7.40
), with the corresponding events defined in (
7.38
), we argue similarly as in the proof of the lower bound. The probability that a single vertex in a size-
\ell
component of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}[1,\phi n)
connects by an edge to each of the at most
h_{\mathrm{up}}\!-\!1
hubs is at most
p
, independently of each other under the conditioning. Hence, the probability that a component of size
\ell
does not connect by an edge to one of the hubs is at least
(1-p)^{\ell(h_{\mathrm{up}}-1)}
, and connected components connect independently by an edge to the hubs. Thus, conditionally on
S_{n,\ell}(\phi n)
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}
,
\overline{T}_{n,\ell}
dominates a binomial random variable with parameters
S_{n,\ell}(\phi n)
and
(1-p)^{\ell(h_{\mathrm{up}}-1)}
. On the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}
,

S_{n,\ell}(\phi n)\geq(1-\psi)n\theta_{\ell}/\ell
, so
\overline{T}_{n,\ell}
dominates a binomial random variable with parameters
(1-\psi)n\theta_{\ell}/\ell
and
(1-p)^{\ell(h_{\mathrm{up}}-1)}
. A union bound over
\ell\leq\ell^{\star}+1
and then a Chernoff bound shows that
\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{conn}}\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{comp}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{hubs}}\big)=\exp\big(-\Theta(n)\big)
. Substituting these three bounds into (
7.40
) finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

2.5
.

Recall
h_{\mathrm{lo}}
and
h_{\mathrm{up}}
from (
7.26
). The lower bound is given by Proposition
7.8
, the upper bound by Proposition
7.9
.
∎

Appendix A
Preliminary lemmas

Lemma A.1

(Concentration of Poisson random variables
[
62
]
)
.

For
x>1
,

(A.1)

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathrm{Poi}}(\lambda)\geq x\lambda\big)\leq\exp(-\lambda(1+x\log(x)-x)),

and for
x<1
,

(A.2)

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathrm{Poi}}(\lambda)\leq x\lambda\big)\leq\exp(-\lambda(1-x-x\log(1/x)).

Appendix B
Existence of a giant: postponed proofs

B.1.
Polynomially-sized components

The proof of
Lemma

4.2
uses a similar strategy as the proof of
Proposition

4.1
: we first prove the statement along a subsequence
(n_{i})_{i\geq 1}
using a multi-scale renormalization method, and then extend the result to all
n
. For the multi-scale renormalization, we follow the steps
(Sub),(Con),(Mark)
outlined in
Section

4.2.1
. The initialization of the renormalization is different. Here, we shall use the uniqueness of the infinite cluster to construct large connected components in level-
0
boxes. We use slightly overlapping boxes, so the events that two overlapping boxes contain a large connected component in their induced subgraph are no longer independent. Therefore, we cannot use Chernoff bounds as we did in the proof of
Proposition

4.1
. Markov’s inequality can still be applied, but it leads to weaker bounds.
We start by formally setting up the renormalization.

We recall the parameter
\beta
from the connectivity function in (
4.6
), and the critical value
\beta_{c}
from (
2.3
).
From
4.5
, we recall that we can construct the vertex set
{\mathcal{V}}
as the union of two independent PPPs
{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}
and
{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}
with respective spatial intensities
q
and
1-q
, with
\beta q^{d}>\beta_{c}
gives two independent PPPs. The choice of
q
ensures that the graph
{\mathcal{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}=({\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}},{\mathcal{E}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}})
induced by vertices in
{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}
is supercritical itself.
Let
\theta^{\mathrm{{\scriptscriptstyle base}}}
be the percolation probability of
{\mathcal{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}
. Lastly, w.l.o.g. we assume that
\alpha<\infty
, as otherwise
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}=(\tau-1)/\alpha-(\tau-2)<0
.

Definition B.1

(renormalization scheme with overlapping boxes)
.

Let
\zeta=\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}>0
, and let
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
be a sufficiently small constant. Let

(B.1)

\eta:=\lceil 2(1/\varepsilon^{\prime}-1)\rceil.

Given
\alpha\in(1,\infty)
, let
s=s(\alpha,\varepsilon^{\prime})
be the smallest integer satisfying
s^{\varepsilon^{\prime}+\alpha}(s+1)^{-\alpha}\geq 1
(so that
\tilde{s}^{(\varepsilon^{\prime}+\alpha)}(\tilde{s}+1)^{-\alpha}\geq 1
for all
\tilde{s}\geq s
).
Let
n_{0}\geq\underline{n}_{0}\geq 1
be two large constants. Then, we iteratively define for
i\geq 1
,

(B.2)

m_{i}:=(i+s)^{(2+\eta)d},\qquad\underline{n}_{i}:=\underline{n}_{i-1}m_{i}=\underline{n}_{0}\prod_{\ell=1}^{i}(\ell+s)^{(2+\eta)d},\qquad n_{i}:=\big(\underline{n}_{i}^{1/d}+n_{0}^{1/d}-\underline{n}_{0}^{1/d}\big)^{d}.

Let
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}(x)
denote boxes of volume
\underline{n}_{i}
and
n_{i}
centered at
x
.
We define a subgraph
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}:=(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{i,x},\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{i,x})
of the graph
{\mathcal{G}}
on
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)
. We call
\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{i,x}
the level-
i
vertex set inside
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}(x)
and define it as

(B.3)

\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{i,x}:={\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}(x)}\cup{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)}[1,2\underline{n}_{i}^{\gamma}).

Let
{\mathcal{E}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}_{i,x}
be the edges of
{\mathcal{G}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}
inside
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}(x)
. Denote then by
{\mathcal{E}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr\textnormal{-}base}}}_{i,x}
the set of edges of
{\mathcal{G}}
inside
Q_{i}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}}(x)
with one endpoint in
{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}[1,2\underline{n}_{i}^{\gamma})
and one in
{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}
, formally
\{\{u,v\}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)}:u\in{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)}[1,2\underline{n}_{i}^{\gamma}),v\in{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)}\}
.
Then we say that the level-
i
edge set inside
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}(x)
is

(B.4)

\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{i,x}:={\mathcal{E}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}_{i,x}\cup{\mathcal{E}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr\textnormal{-}base}}}_{i,x}.

We define the graphs
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}:=(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{i,x},\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{i,x})
.
Let
\rho_{0}:=\theta^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}/2
, and define for
i\geq 1

(B.5)

\rho_{i}:=1/(i+s)^{2d}.

We call a box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}(x)

(i,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-good if the graph
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}
satisfies the event

(B.6)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{i\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}(x):=\Big\{\exists\text{comp.\ ${\mathcal{C}}_{i,x}$ in }\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}

\displaystyle:|{\mathcal{C}}_{i,x}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)|\geq\underline{n}_{i}\prod_{\ell=0}^{i}\rho_{\ell},

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)}[\underline{n}_{i}^{\gamma},2\underline{n}_{i}^{\gamma})\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{i,x}|\geq\underline{n}_{i}^{\zeta-2\varepsilon^{\prime}}\Big\}.

The picture about the boxes
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x),{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}(x)
one should have in mind is as follows:
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}(x)
, the box of volume
n_{i}
, is the “fattening” of
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)
by at most an
n_{0}
-dependent constant. By construction,
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)
can be partitioned into exactly
\underline{n}_{i}/\underline{n}_{0}
many non-overlapping small boxes
({\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{0}(x_{j}))_{j\geq 1}
of volume
\underline{n}_{0}
, centered at some
(x_{j})_{j\geq 1}
. If we take the union of the overlapping fatter boxes
({\mathcal{Q}}_{0}(x_{j}))_{j\geq 1}
of volume
n_{0}>\underline{n}_{0}
, we obtain
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}(x)
.
Since
\underline{n}_{0},n_{0}
are both constants, we obtain that

(B.7)

n_{i}=(1+o(1))\underline{n}_{i},\qquad\text{as }i\to\infty.

The edge set at level
i
consists of all edges in
{\mathcal{E}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}
whose vertices are in the larger box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}(x)
, and additionally, the edges from certain “high-mark vertices” in
{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}
to
{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}
with both endpoints inside the smaller box
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)
.

For the connected component that realizes the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{i\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}(x)
, we have a lower bound on the proportion of vertices inside the smaller box
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)
, and also on the amount of vertices in the range
[\underline{n}_{i}^{\gamma},2\underline{n}_{i}^{\gamma})
in the sprinkled vertex set in
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)
. These criteria in (
B.6
) do not require any information about vertices in the ‘box-annulus’
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}(x)\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)
.

We can compute the density of the component
{\mathcal{C}}_{i,x}
inside the smaller box
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)
. By definition of
\underline{n}_{i}
in (
B.2
) and
\rho_{i}
in (
B.5
), for any
\underline{n}_{0}
sufficiently large, using that
\eta/(\eta+2)\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}
due to (
B.1
), this density is

(B.8)

\prod_{\ell=0}^{i}\rho_{\ell}=\rho_{0}\prod_{\ell=1}^{i}(\ell+s)^{-2d}=\rho_{0}(\underline{n}_{i}/\underline{n}_{0})^{-2/(2+\eta)}\geq(\theta^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}/2)\cdot n_{i}^{-\varepsilon^{\prime}}.

Hence, combined with (
B.7
), for all
i
sufficiently large,

(B.9)

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n_{i}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|\geq n_{i}^{1-\varepsilon^{\prime}}\theta^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}/4\big)\geq\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{i\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}(0)\big).

This is closely related to what we aim for in
Lemma

4.2
. In the next three claims, we will show that the right-hand side can be made arbitrarily close to
1
for all
i
when
\underline{n}_{0}
is sufficiently large. Afterwards we prove
Lemma

4.2
to bound the left-hand side for arbitrary
n
.

Claim B.2

(Ergodicity and uniqueness)
.

Consider a supercritical KSRG as in
Lemma

4.2
and Definition
B.1
. Then the measure
\mathbb{P}
is ergodic, and there is a unique infinite component.

Proof.

We start with ergodicity. We construct the graph as a marked Poisson point process
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
such that the graph remains invariant under translation. These marks are ‘additional’ to the vertex marks
w_{v}
. Namely, we equip each vertex
v=(x_{v},w_{v})\in{\mathcal{V}}
with a sequence of iid
\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]
random variables
(U_{vi})_{i\geq 1}
. Given this marked PPP, we write
{\mathcal{U}}(v)=(u_{i})_{i\geq 1}
for all vertices whose first spatial coordinate is strictly larger than the first spatial coordinate of
v
, increasingly ordered with respect to their Euclidean distance to
v
. We include an edge between
v
and
u_{i}
iff
U_{vi}\leq\mathrm{p}(v,u_{i})
.
This yields a graph that has the same distribution as in
Definition

2.1
. By the adaptation of
[
56
, Proposition 8.13]
to marked Poisson point processes (see also
[
56
, Exercise 10.1]
), such a process is ergodic: any event in the invariant
\sigma
-algebra has either probability
0
or
1
.

The uniqueness of the infinite cluster follows by the classical Burton-Keane argument
[
18
]
, which makes implicit use of ergodicity. We do
not
sketch the Burton-Keane argument here, just highlight one point: the argument requires that the expected size of the vertex boundary of
\Lambda_{n}
(i.e., the number of vertices in
\Lambda_{n}
with an edge leaving
\Lambda_{n}
) is of smaller order than the total number of vertices inside
\Lambda_{n}
. Formally, we have to show that

(B.10)

\mathrm{VB}_{n}:=\mathbb{E}\big[|\{v\in\Lambda_{n}:v\text{ connected by an edge to }\Lambda_{n}^{\mathsf{c}}\}|\big]

is
o(n)
. Fix
\varepsilon>0
arbitrarily small, and let
\delta=\delta(\varepsilon)
be a small constant depending on
\varepsilon
, such that the expected number of vertices in
\Lambda_{n}
within distance
\delta n^{1/d}
from the boundary
\partial\Lambda_{n}
is at most
\varepsilon n
. Since
\varepsilon>0
is arbitrary, we may bound from above the vertex boundary in (
B.10
) by counting all these vertices in
\mathrm{VB}_{n}
. The remaining vertices are in
\Lambda_{n(1-\delta)^{d}}
, and they only contribute to the vertex boundary in (
B.10
) if they have an edge of length
\delta n^{1/d}
. Thus,

(B.11)

\mathrm{VB}_{n}\leq\varepsilon n+\mathbb{E}\big[|\{v\in\Lambda_{n}:v\text{ incident to edge of length at least $\delta n^{1/d}$}\}|\big].

We use translation invariance of the graph and Mecke’s formula, and arrive at

\mathrm{VB}_{n}\leq\varepsilon n+n(1-\delta)^{d}\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(0\text{ incident to edge of length at least $\delta n^{1/d}$}\big).

When
\alpha>1
and
\tau>2
in
Assumption

2.2
, the graph is locally finite, see also
[
27
,
44
,
61
]
, which implies that the probability on the left-hand side tends to
0
as
n\to\infty
. Therefore, the expected size of the vertex boundary is
o(n)
. By the Burton-Keane argument, the uniqueness of the infinite cluster follows.
∎

Claim B.3

(Induction base for overlapping boxes)
.

Consider a KSRG in the setting of
Lemma

4.2
and Definition
B.1
. Let
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
is a sufficiently small constant. For each
\delta>0
, there exists a constant
\underline{n}_{0}^{\star}>0
such that for each
\underline{n}_{0}\geq\underline{n}_{0}^{\star}
there exists
n_{0}>0
such that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{Q}}_{0}(x)\text{ is $(0,\varepsilon^{\prime})$-good}\big)\geq 1-\delta.

Proof.

We estimate the probability of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{0\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}(x)
defined in (
B.6
). We set
x=0
, so that
{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}}(0)=\Lambda_{\underline{n}_{0}}
while
{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}(0)=\Lambda_{n_{0}}
. We write
{\mathcal{C}}_{\infty}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}
for the unique infinite component of
{\mathcal{G}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}
. By definition,
\theta^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}=\mathbb{P}(0\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\infty}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}})
, so that
{\mathcal{C}}_{\infty}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}
typically contains a
\theta^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}
-proportion of the vertices in
\Lambda_{\underline{n}_{0}}
by translation invariance. By ergodicity (
Claim

B.2
), the proportion of vertices in
\Lambda_{\underline{n}_{0}}
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{\infty}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}
converges in probability to
\theta^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}
as
\underline{n}_{0}\to\infty
. In
Definition

B.1
we defined
\rho_{0}=\theta^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}/2
.
Hence, for
\underline{n}_{0}
sufficiently large,

\mathbb{P}\big(|\Lambda_{\underline{n}_{0}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\infty}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}|\geq\rho_{0}\underline{n}_{0}\big)\geq 1-\delta/4.

Since the infinite component is unique (
Claim

B.2
) and the graph is locally finite, given
\delta
, there exists
n_{0}=n_{0}(\underline{n}_{0})
such that with probability at least
1-\delta/2
, the separate components that
{\mathcal{C}}_{\infty}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}
induces inside
\Lambda_{\underline{n}_{0}}
are all in a single component inside the larger box
\Lambda_{n_{0}}
. Formally, let
n_{0}
be so large that

(B.12)

\mathbb{P}\big(|\Lambda_{\underline{n}_{0}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\infty}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}|\geq\rho_{0}\underline{n}_{0},\Lambda_{\underline{n}_{0}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\infty}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}\mbox{ in same component in }{\mathcal{G}}_{n_{0}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}\big)\geq 1-\delta/2

holds. This also implies that the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{base}}:=\{\exists\text{ comp. }{\mathcal{C}}\text{ in }{\mathcal{G}}_{n_{0}}:|{\mathcal{C}}\cap\Lambda_{\underline{n}_{0}}|\geq\rho_{0}\underline{n}_{0}\}
occurs with probability at least
1-\delta/2
.
We now condition on the realization of the graph
{\mathcal{G}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}
satisfying
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{base}}
.
Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be an arbitrary component in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n_{0}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}
that the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{base}}
describes. This component only uses vertices and edges of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n_{0}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}
, which is also a subgraph of the graph
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{0,x}
. The component
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\supseteq{\mathcal{C}}
in
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,0}
thus satisfies the first condition of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{0\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}
in (
B.6
). For the second criterion of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{0\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}
we will compute how many vertices of
{\mathcal{V}}_{\underline{n}_{0}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}[\underline{n}_{0}^{\gamma},2\underline{n}_{0}^{\gamma})
connect to
{\mathcal{C}}
(and thus form
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}
). Since we revealed a realization of
{\mathcal{G}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}}
satisfying
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{base}}
,
{\mathcal{C}}
is known, and we obtain

\mathds{1}_{\{{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{base}}\}}\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{0\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}(x)\mid{\mathcal{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}\big)\leq\mathds{1}_{\{{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{base}}\}}\mathbb{P}\big(|\{u\in{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}[\underline{n}_{0}^{\gamma},2\underline{n}_{0}^{\gamma}):u\sim{\mathcal{C}}\}|<\underline{n}_{0}^{\zeta-2\varepsilon^{\prime}}\mid{\mathcal{G}}^{\mathrm{base}}\big).

The vertices in
{\mathcal{V}}_{\underline{n}_{0}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}[\underline{n}_{0}^{\gamma},2\underline{n}_{0}^{\gamma})
form a PPP with intensity
(1-q)\mathrm{Leb}(\cdot)\times(\tau-1)w^{-\tau}\mathrm{d}w
. We compute the mean and estimate it from below using that
1-\gamma(\tau-1)=\zeta
:

\mathbb{E}\big[|{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}_{\underline{n}_{0}}[\underline{n}_{0}^{\gamma},2\underline{n}_{0}^{\gamma})|\big]\geq(1-q)\underline{n}_{0}^{1-\gamma(\tau-1)}(1-2^{-(\tau-1)})\geq\underline{n}_{0}^{\zeta-\varepsilon^{\prime}}.

We estimate the mark of each vertex in
{\mathcal{C}}\cap\Lambda_{\underline{n}_{0}}
from below by one, the spatial distance by at most the diameter of
\Lambda_{\underline{n}_{0}}
, and the size of
{\mathcal{C}}\cap\Lambda_{\underline{n}_{0}}
by
\rho_{0}\underline{n}_{0}
. So, a single vertex in
{\mathcal{V}}_{\underline{n}_{0}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}[\underline{n}_{0}^{\gamma},2\underline{n}_{0}^{\gamma})
connects to
{\mathcal{C}}
with probability at least

\displaystyle 1-\Big(1-p\Big(1\wedge\beta\frac{\underline{n}_{0}^{\gamma}}{d^{d/2}\underline{n}_{0}}\Big)^{\alpha}\Big)^{\rho_{0}\underline{n}_{0}}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\exp\big(-p\beta^{\alpha}\rho_{0}d^{-\alpha d/2}\underline{n}_{0}^{\gamma\alpha-\alpha+1}\big)

\displaystyle=1-\exp\big(-p\beta^{\alpha}\rho_{0}d^{-\alpha d/2}\big)\geq 2\underline{n}_{0}^{-\varepsilon^{\prime}}.

Here we used that
\gamma=1-1/\alpha
to obtain the second row so that powers of
\underline{n}_{0}
vanish, and that the last inequality holds for all
\underline{n}_{0}
sufficiently large. Given the vertex set, vertices in
{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}
connect independently by an edge to
{\mathcal{C}}
. So, the total number of vertices in
{\mathcal{V}}_{\underline{n}_{0}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}[\underline{n}_{i}^{\gamma},2\underline{n}_{i}^{\gamma})
dominates a Poisson random variable with mean
2\underline{n}_{0}^{\zeta-2\varepsilon^{\prime}}
. By concentration for Poisson random variables (
Lemma

A.1
), there exists
c>0
such that

\mathds{1}_{\{{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{base}}\}}\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{Q}}_{0}(x)\text{ is $(0,\varepsilon^{\prime})$-bad}\big)\leq\exp\big(-c\underline{n}_{0}^{\zeta-2\varepsilon^{\prime}}\big).

The right-hand side is at most
\delta/2
if
\underline{n}_{0}
is large. Together with (
B.12
), this finishes the proof.
∎

The key idea of
Claim

B.3
above is that the separate components of the infinite component in
{\mathcal{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}
induced in a box
\Lambda_{\underline{n}_{0}}
connect to each other in a larger box
\Lambda_{n_{0}}
. However, we had no control over the relation between
n_{0}
and
\underline{n}_{0}
, and thus lose the density estimate of the connected component in the larger box. The multi-scale renormalization in the following claim makes this control possible, using that the added annulus in the ‘fattened’ box has constant width across levels, see (
B.7
).

Claim B.4

(Iterative renormalization with overlapping boxes)
.

Consider a KSRG in the setting of
Lemma

4.2
, with the boxing scheme from
Definition

B.1
. Assume
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
is a sufficiently small constant. For each
\delta>0
, there exists a constant
\underline{n}_{0}^{\star}=\underline{n}_{0}^{\star}(\varepsilon^{\prime},\delta)>0
such that for each
\underline{n}_{0}\geq\underline{n}_{0}^{\star}
there exists
n_{0}>0
so that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and all
i\geq 0
,

(B.13)

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{Q}}_{i}(x)\text{ is $(i,\varepsilon^{\prime})$-good}\big)\geq 1-\delta.

Proof.

We follow the same steps
(Sub), (Con)
, and
(Mark)
, similar to the proof of
Claim

4.11
. By
Claim

B.3
the statement holds for
i=0
, so we may assume inductively that it holds until
i-1
and that
i\geq 1
.

Good subboxes.

Consider
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}(x)
, and take a partition of the smaller box
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)
into
m_{i}
many subboxes of volume
\underline{n}_{i-1}
with centers
(x_{j})_{j\leq m_{i}}
. We then obtain
\big(({\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i-1}(x_{j}),{\mathcal{Q}}_{i-1}(x_{j}))\big)_{j\leq m_{i}}
, where
({\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i-1}(x_{j}))_{j\leq m_{i}}
are the subboxes forming the partitioning, and
({\mathcal{Q}}_{i-1}(x_{j}))_{j\leq m_{i}}
their fattened versions. Since two neighboring fattened subboxes overlap, the events that two fattened boxes are
(i-1,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-good are not independent. Let
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}
be the event that at least
\rho_{i}m_{i}
fattened boxes are
(i-1,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-good.
By Markov’s inequality and translation invariance,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\big(|\{j:{\mathcal{Q}}_{i-1}(x_{j})\text{ is $(i,\varepsilon^{\prime})$-bad}\}|\geq(1-\rho_{i})m_{i}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\rho_{i}}\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{Q}}_{i-1}(0)\text{ is $(i,\varepsilon^{\prime})$-bad}\big)

(B.14)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1+\frac{2}{(i+s)^{2d}}\Big)\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{Q}}_{i-1}(0)\text{ is $(i,\varepsilon^{\prime})$-bad}\big),

where for the second bound we used
\rho_{i}=(i+s)^{-2d}
, so that
1/(1-\rho_{i})\leq 1+2(i+s)^{-2d}
for all
i\geq 1
and
d\geq 1
.

Connection between subboxes.

We write
\mathrm{pre}
-
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}
for the union of the graphs
\cup_{j\leq m_{i}}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i-1,x_{j}}
inside the subboxes at level
i-1
, see (
B.3
) and (
B.4
), and assume that
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}
holds. Let
\mathrm{inter}
-
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}
denote the graph on the same vertex set as
\mathrm{pre}
-
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}
, but including also all edges between the vertices in
\cup_{j\leq m_{i}}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{i-1,j}
.

The auxiliary graph.

Similar to below (
4.31
), we re-label the subboxes so that the subboxes with indices
j\leq\rho_{i}m_{i}
are all
(i-1,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-good. We consider the auxiliary graph
{\mathcal{H}}_{i}=({\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{i}},{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{i}})
: each vertex
j\in[\rho_{i}m_{i}]=:{\mathcal{V}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{i}}
corresponds to a component
{\mathcal{C}}_{i-1,x_{j}}
that realizes the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{i\textnormal{-}\mathrm{good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}(x_{j})
in the
j
-th subbox, see (
B.6
). To determine the edges of the auxiliary graph, we use the vertices at level
i-1
that belong to inter-
{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}
as follows: we connect vertex
j
and
k
in
{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
if there is an edge between a sprinkled vertex in
{\mathcal{C}}_{i-1,j}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}_{i-1}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}}(x_{j})
to some base-vertex in
{\mathcal{C}}_{i-1,k}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}_{i-1}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}}(x_{k})
or vice versa:

\displaystyle\{j\sim_{{\mathcal{H}}_{i}}k\}\ \Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\Big\{\big({\mathcal{C}}_{i-1,j}\cap{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i-1}(x_{j})}[\underline{n}_{i-1}^{\gamma},2\underline{n}_{i-1}^{\gamma})\big)\sim_{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}}\big({\mathcal{C}}_{i-1,k}\cap{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i-1}(x_{k})}\big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\cup\,\Big\{\big({\mathcal{C}}_{i-1,k}\cap{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i-1}(x_{k})}[\underline{n}_{i-1}^{\gamma},2\underline{n}_{i-1}^{\gamma})\big)\sim_{\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}}\big({\mathcal{C}}_{i-1,j}\cap{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i-1}(x_{j})}\big)\Big\}.

Each good subbox
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i-1}(x_{j})
is contained in
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)
, and the connected component
{\mathcal{C}}_{i-1,j}
satisfies
|{\mathcal{C}}_{i-1,j}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i-1}(x_{j})|\geq\underline{n}_{i-1}\prod_{\ell=0}^{i-1}\rho_{\ell}
. On
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}
there are at least
\rho_{i}m_{i}
good subboxes. Thus, if
{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
is connected, then there is a component
{\mathcal{C}}_{i,x}
satisfying:

|{\mathcal{C}}_{i,x}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)|\geq\rho_{i}m_{i}\cdot\underline{n}_{i-1}\prod_{\ell=0}^{i-1}\rho_{\ell}=\underline{n}_{i}\prod_{\ell=0}^{i}\rho_{\ell}.

We bound from above the probability that
{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
is not connected by the probability that
{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
is not the complete graph. We already lost sharp bounds in (
B.14
) as opposed to (
4.32
) and (
4.35
) where we used the connectivity of the Erdős-Rényi random graph. So even though the edges in
{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
are conditionally independent given pre-
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}
, we will use Markov’s inequality to bound edge-presence events.
We compute conditionally on the realization of
\mathrm{pre}\textnormal{-}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}
, the union of subgraphs inside level-(
i-1
)-subboxes that

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}\}}\mathbb{P}\bigg(\nexists\text{component }{\mathcal{C}}_{i,x}\text{ in }\mathrm{inter}\textnormal{-}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}

\displaystyle:|{\mathcal{C}}_{i,x}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)|\geq\underline{n}_{i}\prod_{\ell=0}^{i}\rho_{\ell}\,\Big|\,\mathrm{pre}\textnormal{-}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathds{1}_{\{{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}\}}\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{H}}_{i}\mbox{ is not connected}\mid\mathrm{pre}\textnormal{-}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}\big)

\displaystyle\leq m_{i}^{2}\max_{j,k}\mathds{1}_{\{{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}\}}\mathbb{P}\big(j\nsim_{{\mathcal{H}}_{i}}k\mid\mathrm{pre}\textnormal{-}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}\big).

To bound the probability on the right-hand side, we use the two properties of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i-1,j}
of a good
(i-1,\varepsilon^{\prime})
subbox in (
B.6
) about its density in
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i-1}(x_{j})
and its level-
(i-1)
high-mark vertices.
Since the union of the smaller level-
(i-1)
-boxes forms
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}(x)
(see reasoning below
Definition

B.1
), the maximal distance between two vertices in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i-1}(x_{j})
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i-1}(x_{k})
is
\sqrt{d}\underline{n}_{i}^{1/d}
.
Moreover, by the definition of the level-
(i-1)
edge set in (
B.4
), none of the edges between
{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{i-1}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}(x_{j})}
and
{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{i-1}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}(x_{k})}
have been revealed in pre-
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}
and they are thus conditionally independently present. We bound this probability from above by only requiring that there is no edge between
{\mathcal{C}}_{i-1,j}\cap{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{i-1}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}(x_{j})}[\underline{n}_{i-1}^{\gamma},2\underline{n}_{i-1}^{\gamma})
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{i-1,k}\cap{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{i-1}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}(x_{k})}
, and we use that we have lower bounds on the sizes of these sets in (
B.6
):

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}\}}\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{H}}_{i}\mbox{ is not connected}

\displaystyle\mid\mathrm{pre\textnormal{-}}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}\big)

\displaystyle\leq m_{i}^{2}\prod_{u\in{\mathcal{C}}_{i-1,j}\cap{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i-1}(x_{j_{1}})}[\underline{n}_{i-1}^{\gamma},2\underline{n}_{i-1}^{\gamma})}\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{C}}_{i-1,k}\cap{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{base}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i-1}(x_{j_{2}})}}(1-\mathrm{p}(u,v))

\displaystyle\leq m_{i}^{2}\Big(1-p\Big(1\wedge\beta\frac{\underline{n}_{i-1}^{\gamma}}{d^{d/2}\underline{n}_{i}}\Big)^{\alpha}\Big)^{\underline{n}_{i-1}^{1+\zeta-2\varepsilon^{\prime}}\prod_{\ell=0}^{i-1}\rho_{\ell}}.

We use that
\underline{n}_{i}=m_{i}\underline{n}_{i-1}
and
\gamma=1-1/\alpha
, so that the minimum is attained at the second term for any
n_{0}
sufficiently large. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}\}}\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{H}}_{i}\mbox{ is not connected}\mid\mathrm{pre\textnormal{-}}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}\big)

\displaystyle\leq m_{i}^{2}\exp\Big(-p\beta^{\alpha}m_{i}^{-\alpha}\underline{n}_{i-1}^{(\gamma-1)\alpha}\underline{n}_{i-1}^{\zeta+1-2\varepsilon^{\prime}}\prod_{\ell=0}^{i-1}\rho_{\ell}\Big)

(B.15)

\displaystyle=m_{i}^{2}\exp\Big(-m_{i}^{-\alpha}\underline{n}_{i-1}^{\zeta-2\varepsilon^{\prime}}p\beta^{\alpha}\prod_{\ell=0}^{i-1}\rho_{\ell}\Big).

The expression
\prod_{\ell=0}^{i-1}\rho_{\ell}
inside the exponent is at least
\underline{n}_{i}^{-\varepsilon^{\prime}}
by (
B.8
).
We would also like to replace the factor
m_{i}^{-\alpha}
by
\underline{n}_{i-1}^{-\varepsilon^{\prime}}
. For this it is enough to show that
\underline{n}_{i-1}^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\geq m_{i}^{\alpha}
with
m_{i}
and
\underline{n}_{i}
from (
B.2
). We show this by induction. For
i=1
,
m_{1}^{\alpha}=(1+s)^{(2+\eta)d\alpha}\leq\underline{n}_{0}^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}
holds for any
\underline{n}_{0}
sufficiently large. We advance the induction, assuming
\underline{n}_{i-2}^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\geq m_{i-1}^{\alpha}
. Using the recursion defining
\underline{n}_{i}
and the induction hypothesis,

\underline{n}_{i-1}^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}=m_{i-1}^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\underline{n}_{i-2}^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\geq m_{i-1}^{\varepsilon^{\prime}+\alpha}=m_{i}^{\alpha}\Big(\frac{m_{i-1}}{m_{i}}\Big)^{\alpha}m_{i-1}^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}=m_{i}^{\alpha}\Big(\frac{i-1+s}{i+s}\Big)^{(2+\eta)d\alpha}(i-1+s)^{(2+\eta)d\varepsilon^{\prime}}\geq m_{i}^{\alpha},

by the choice of
s
just above (
B.2
).
Substituting this bound into (
B.15
), and discounting the prefactor
m_{i-1}^{2}
and constant factors inside the exponential by another factor
\underline{n}_{i-1}^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}
, we obtain

(B.16)

\mathds{1}_{\{{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}\}}\mathbb{P}\bigg(\nexists\text{comp. }{\mathcal{C}}_{i}\text{ in }\mathrm{inter\textnormal{-}}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}:|{\mathcal{C}}_{i}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)|\geq\underline{n}_{i}\prod_{\ell=0}^{i}\rho_{\ell}\,\Big|\,\mathrm{pre\textnormal{-}}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}\bigg)\leq\exp\big(-\underline{n}_{i-1}^{\zeta-5\varepsilon^{\prime}}\big).

Sprinkling high-mark vertices.
Let us denote the opposite event of (
B.16
) by

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{conn}}:=\Big\{\exists\text{comp. }{\mathcal{C}}_{i}\text{ in }\mathrm{inter\textnormal{-}}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}:|{\mathcal{C}}_{i}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)|\geq\underline{n}_{i}\prod_{\ell=0}^{i}\rho_{\ell}\Big\}

.
On
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{conn}}
,
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}
satisfies the first criterion of being
(i,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-good in (
B.6
). We now look at the second criterion of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{i\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}(0)
in (
B.6
), conditioned on the graph
\mathrm{inter}\textnormal{-}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}
satisfying the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{conn}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}
. We reveal the sprinkled vertices
{\mathcal{V}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}}[\underline{n}_{i}^{\gamma},2\underline{n}_{i}^{\gamma})
with location in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}(x)
. These vertices are independent of
\mathrm{inter\textnormal{-}}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}
, which only contains vertices with mark below
2\underline{n}_{i-1}^{\gamma}
. This mark threshold is below
\underline{n}_{i}^{\gamma}
for sufficiently large
\underline{n}_{0}
and small
\varepsilon^{\prime}
see (
B.1
–
B.2
). The expected number of revealed vertices is then
(1-q)\underline{n}_{i}^{1-\gamma(\tau-1)}(1-2^{-(\tau-1)})
using
Definition

4.5
.

We bound from below the probability that such a vertex connects by an edge to one of the at least
\underline{n}_{i}\prod_{\ell=0}^{i}\rho_{\ell}
many vertices in
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)
of
\mathrm{inter}\textnormal{-}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}
. The probability that a single vertex of mark at least
\underline{n}_{i}^{\gamma}
does not connect by an edge to
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
is at most

\Big(1-p\Big(1\wedge\beta\frac{\underline{n}_{i}^{\gamma}}{d^{d/2}\underline{n}_{i}}\Big)^{\alpha}\Big)^{\underline{n}_{i}\prod_{\ell=0}^{i}\rho_{\ell}}\leq\exp\Big(-p\beta^{\alpha}d^{-\alpha d/2}\underline{n}_{i}^{(\gamma-1)\alpha+1}\prod_{\ell=0}^{i}\rho_{\ell}\Big)=\exp\Big(-p\beta^{\alpha}d^{-\alpha d/2}\prod_{\ell=0}^{i}\rho_{\ell}\Big),

using again
\gamma=1-1/\alpha
and cancellation of the powers of
\underline{n}_{i}
. Each vertex in
{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}
connects by an edge independently to the vertices in
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
. Thus, the total number of vertices from
{\mathcal{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{spr}}[\underline{n}_{i}^{\gamma},2\underline{n}_{i}^{\gamma})\cap{\mathcal{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{sub}}_{i}(x)
connecting to
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
dominates a Poisson random variable with mean

(1-q)(1-2^{-(\tau-1)})\underline{n}_{i}^{\zeta}\cdot\Big(1-\exp\Big(-p\beta^{\alpha}d^{-\alpha d/2}\prod_{\ell=0}^{i}\rho_{\ell}\Big)\Big)\geq c\underline{n}_{i}^{\zeta}\prod_{\ell=0}^{i}\rho_{\ell}\geq c\rho_{0}\underline{n}_{i}^{\zeta-\varepsilon^{\prime}}\geq 2\underline{n}_{i}^{\zeta-2\varepsilon^{\prime}},

where the second bound follows for some constant
c>0
, the third bound by (
B.8
), and the last bound if
\underline{n}_{0}
is sufficiently large. We apply concentration for Poisson random variables (
Lemma

A.1
). We recall the definition of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{i\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}
from (
B.6
), and combine it with (
B.16
) to obtain for
\underline{n}_{0}
sufficiently large that

\mathds{1}_{\{{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{conn}}\}}\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{i\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}(0)\big)\leq 2\exp\big(-\underline{n}_{i}^{\zeta-4\varepsilon^{\prime}}\mid\mathrm{inter}\textnormal{-}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i,x}\big).

We combine this bound with
\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}\big)\leq\big(1+2(i+s)^{-2d}\big)\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{(i-1)\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}(0)\big)
from (
B.14
). Then

(B.17)

\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{i\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}(0)\big)\leq\big(1+2(i+s)^{-2d}\big)\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{(i-1)\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}(0)\big)+2\exp\big(-\underline{n}_{i}^{\zeta-5\varepsilon^{\prime}}\big).

Combining everything: a recursive bound.

We finish the proof of (
B.13
) by some analysis.
We show that the right-hand side of (
B.17
) is at most
\delta
by working out the recursion in
i
.
Fix
\delta^{\prime}>0
sufficiently small.
By
Claim

B.3
,
\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{0\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}(0)\big)\leq\delta^{\prime}
for all
\underline{n}_{0}
sufficiently large. We may assume inductively that for all
1\leq j\leq i-1
,

\mathbb{P}(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{j\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}(0))\leq\delta^{\prime}\prod_{\ell=1}^{j}\Big(1+\frac{3}{(\ell+s)^{2d}}\Big).

Then, using this bound in (
B.17
) we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{i\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}(0))

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{\prime}\Big(1+\frac{2}{(i+s)^{2d}}\Big)\prod_{\ell=1}^{i-1}\Big(1+\frac{3}{(\ell+s)^{2d}}\Big)+2\exp\big(-\underline{n}_{i}^{\zeta-5\varepsilon^{\prime}}\big).

Showing that the right-hand side is at most
\delta^{\prime}\prod_{\ell=1}^{i}\big(1+\frac{3}{(\ell+s)^{2d}}\big)
is equivalent to showing that

(B.18)

1+\frac{2}{(i+s)^{2d}}+\frac{2\exp\big(-\underline{n}_{i}^{\zeta-5\varepsilon^{\prime}}\big)}{\delta^{\prime}\prod_{\ell=1}^{i-1}(1+3/(\ell+s)^{2d})}\leq 1+\frac{3}{(i+s)^{2d}}.

The product in the denominator on the left-hand side is bounded away from 0. Since
\underline{n}_{i}
grows superpolynomially by its definition in (
B.2
), the bound (
B.18
) is satisfied for all
i\geq 1
if
\underline{n}_{0}
is sufficiently large. This advances the induction, and we conclude that for all
i\geq 0
and a proper choice of the constant
\delta^{\prime}>0
,

\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{i\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}(0)\big)\leq\delta^{\prime}\prod_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\Big(1+\frac{3}{(\ell+s)^{2d}}\Big)=\delta.\qed

We now prove
Lemma

4.2
. Similarly to the proof of Proposition
4.1
on page
4.2.4
, this step generalizes the previous claim to arbitrary box size
n
(instead of
n
restricted along the subsequence
(n_{i})_{i\geq 1}
).

Proof of
Lemma

4.2
.

Fix
\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,\varepsilon/2)
. Let
c>0
be a small constant. It suffices to show that for all
\delta
, there exists
n_{\ast}
such that for all
n\geq n_{\ast}
,

(B.19)

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|<n^{1-2\varepsilon^{\prime}}\big)\leq\delta.

Consider the sequence
(n_{i})_{i\geq 0}
from
Definition

B.1
, with
n_{0}
given by
Claim

B.4
such that

(B.20)

\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{i\textnormal{-}good},\varepsilon^{\prime}}(x)\big)\leq\delta/4.

for all
i\geq 0
. Assume
n\gg n_{0}
, and define
m
,
i_{\ast}
, and
\tilde{n}
as in (
4.39
), i.e.,

(B.21)

\displaystyle i_{\ast}=\max\{i:n_{i}<n\},\qquad m:=\lfloor(n/n_{i_{\ast}})^{1/d}\rfloor^{d},\qquad\tilde{n}:=n_{i_{\ast}}m.

We will assume that
i_{\ast}
is a sufficiently large constant.
We consider a box
\Lambda_{\tilde{n}}
of volume
\tilde{n}
inside
\Lambda_{n}
and partition this smaller box into
disjoint
subboxes of volume
n_{i_{\ast}}
. By construction,
\Lambda_{\tilde{n}}
covers a uniformly positive proportion of the larger box
\Lambda_{n}
, i.e., exactly as in (
4.40
), there exists an absolute constant
\nu>0
such that

(B.22)

m\geq\nu(n/n_{i_{\ast}}).

Moreover, we may assume without loss of generality that
m
is at least a large constant
m_{0}
, as otherwise the statement is immediately implied by (
B.9
) and
Claim

B.4
.
We denote the boxes of the partition of
\Lambda_{\tilde{n}}
by
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i_{\ast}}(x_{1}),\ldots,{\mathcal{Q}}_{i_{\ast}}(x_{m})
. By (
B.20
) and Markov’s inequality,

(B.23)

\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}):=\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sum_{j\in[m]}\mathds{1}_{\{{\mathcal{Q}}_{i_{\ast}}(x_{j})\text{ is $(i_{\ast},\varepsilon^{\prime})$-good}\}}\geq m/2\bigg)\geq 1-\delta/2.

We reveal the subgraphs
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{i_{\ast},x_{j}}
from
Definition

B.1
in the subboxes, and denote their union by pre-
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\tilde{n}}
. We condition the graph on satisfying
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{sub}}
: each good subbox contains a large component
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
of size at least
n_{i_{\ast}}^{1-\varepsilon^{\prime}}
. If the components
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
are all connected by an edge to each other, then
{\mathcal{G}}_{\tilde{n}}
contains a connected component of size at least

(1/2)m\underline{n}_{i_{\ast}}\prod_{\ell=1}^{i_{\ast}}\rho_{\ell}\geq(1/2)m\underline{n}_{i_{\ast}}^{1-\varepsilon^{\prime}}\geq(1/4)mn_{i_{\ast}}^{1-\varepsilon^{\prime}}\geq(\nu/4)nn_{i_{\ast}}^{-\varepsilon^{\prime}}\geq n^{1-2\varepsilon^{\prime}}

using (
B.8
), (
B.22
), and the fact that
n_{i}=\underline{n}_{i}(1+o(1))
as
i\to\infty
for all
n
is sufficiently large.

We thus need to study the connectivity between the large connected components
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
in the good subboxes. By the definition of
(i,\varepsilon^{\prime})
goodness in (
B.6
),
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
has size at least
n_{i_{\ast}}^{1-\varepsilon^{\prime}}
, and contains at least
\underline{n}_{i_{\ast}}^{\zeta-2\varepsilon^{\prime}}
vertices of mark at least
\underline{n}_{i_{\ast}}^{\gamma}
. The edges between subboxes have not yet been revealed. The largest possible distance between two vertices in
\Lambda_{\tilde{n}}
is
\sqrt{d}\tilde{n}^{1/d}=\sqrt{d}\tilde{mn_{i_{\ast}}}^{1/d}
. Thus,
the probability that the large components
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{k}
in two good subboxes
j
,
k
are not connected by an edge in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
is at most

\displaystyle\prod_{u\in{\mathcal{C}}_{j},v\in{\mathcal{C}}_{k}}\hskip-10.0pt\Big(1-\mathrm{p}(u,v)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1-p\Big(\beta\frac{\underline{n}_{i_{\ast}}^{\gamma}}{d^{d/2}n_{i_{\ast}}m}\Big)^{\alpha}\Big)^{2\underline{n}_{i_{\ast}}^{1+\zeta-3\varepsilon^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(-p\beta^{\alpha}d^{-\alpha d/2}\underline{n}_{i_{\ast}}^{\gamma\alpha+1-\alpha+\zeta-3\varepsilon^{\prime}}m^{-\alpha}\Big)\leq\exp\Big(-\underline{n}_{i_{\ast}}^{\zeta-4\varepsilon^{\prime}}m^{-\alpha}\Big),

having used
\gamma=1-1/\alpha
in the last step, combined with a bound of
\underline{n}_{i}^{-\varepsilon^{\prime}}
to account for the constant prefactors.
Considering the at most
m^{2}
potential edges, and combining it with (
B.23
), we obtain

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|<n^{1-2\varepsilon^{\prime}}\big)\leq\delta/2+m^{2}\exp\Big(-\underline{n}_{i_{\ast}}^{\zeta-4\varepsilon^{\prime}}m^{-\alpha}\Big).

By (
B.21
),
m
is at most
m_{i_{\ast}}
defined in (
B.2
). By the same reasoning as below (
B.15
), it follows that
m^{-\alpha}\geq\underline{n}_{i_{\ast}}^{-\varepsilon}
. Thus, if
\underline{n}_{0}
is a sufficiently large constant, the second term on the right-hand side is smaller than
\delta/2
. This finishes the proof.
∎

B.2.
Useful bounds for the iterative renormalization

Proof of
Claim

4.9
.

We first discuss (
4.19
). Its proof is standard and based on simple estimates relating to integer parts and induction, so we leave it to the reader.

For the first inequality (Sub) in (
4.16
) we substitute
n_{i}=m_{i}n_{i-1}
on its right-hand side. Simplification gives that (Sub)-inequality is equivalent to

\varepsilon_{i}m_{i}^{1-\zeta+\delta}\geq C

for all
i\geq 1
.
This follows for any sufficiently large
n_{0}
since
m_{i}
is doubly exponentially increasing,
\varepsilon_{i}=(i+1)^{-2}
, and
1-\zeta=\gamma(\tau-1)>0
. For the second inequality (Con) in (
4.17
) we define the constant

\widetilde{C}:=C/\prod_{j=1}^{\infty}(1-\varepsilon_{j})=C/\prod_{j=2}^{\infty}(1-1/j^{2}),

which does not depend on
n_{0}
. It is elementary to check that
C/\rho_{i-1}^{2}\leq\widetilde{C}n_{0}^{\delta/2}
by definition of
\rho_{i}
in (
4.14
).
Hence, dividing both sides with
\rho_{i-1}^{2}
in (
4.17
), and using this inequality, it is sufficient to show that for all
n_{0}
sufficiently large

m_{i}^{1-\alpha}n_{i-1}^{\zeta}\geq\widetilde{C}n_{0}^{\delta/2}n_{i}^{\zeta-\delta}.

Using that
n_{i}=m_{i}n_{i-1}
, after rearranging this is equivalent to
n_{i-1}^{\delta}\geq\widetilde{C}n_{0}^{\delta/2}m_{i}^{\zeta-\delta+\alpha-1}
.
By (
4.11
),
m_{i}\leq n_{i-1}^{\xi_{\delta}}
with
\xi_{\delta}=(\delta/2)/(\zeta+\alpha-1-\delta/2)
, and raising then both sides to the power
2/\delta
gives that (
4.17
) holds if

n_{i-1}^{2}\geq{\widetilde{C}}^{2/\delta}n_{0}n_{i-1}^{1-\xi_{\delta}}
. This inequality holds for all
i\geq 1
when
n_{0}
is sufficiently large (the choice of
n_{0}
depends on
\widetilde{C}
).

For the bound (Mark) in (
4.18
) we have to show that
\rho_{i}n_{i}^{\delta}\geq C
.
The infinite product of
(1-1/j^{2})
is strictly positive in (
4.14
), so
\rho_{i}=\Theta(\rho_{0})=\Theta\big(n_{0}^{-\delta/4}\big)
as
n_{0}\to\infty
. Since
(n_{i})_{i\geq 0}
is increasing, (Mark) follows again for sufficiently large
n_{0}
.

The bounds in (
4.20
) follow since
\varepsilon_{i}=(i+1)^{-2}
for
i\geq 1
and
m_{i}
and
n_{i}
increase doubly exponentially in
i
, while the powers appearing in (
4.20
) are all positive (e.g.
1-\zeta-\delta>0
). The same is true for (
4.22
).
We turn to the last bound (
4.21
). We substitute
n=n_{i}m
and rearrange to obtain that (
4.21
) is equivalent to
n_{i}^{\delta}\geq m^{\zeta-\delta+\alpha-1}
. Since
m\leq m_{i+1}
by assumption, this inequality holds for all such
m
if
n_{i}^{\delta}\geq m_{i+1}^{\zeta-\delta+\alpha-1}
. Since
m_{i+1}\leq n_{i}^{\xi_{\delta}}
by (
4.11
), it follows by definition of
\xi_{\delta}
in (
4.8
) that this last inequality holds if
n_{i}^{\delta}\geq n_{i}^{\delta/2-\xi_{\delta}\delta/2}
, which clearly holds since
\xi_{\delta}>0
.
∎

B.3.
Poly-logarithmic mark-thresholds to belong to the giant

Proof of
Lemma

5.6
.

High-low regime
. Assuming (
5.34
), we will adapt the proof of the upper bound on
|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|
from Section
6
to show (
5.35
) and (
5.36
). Let
\zeta=\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}
,
\gamma=\gamma_{\mathrm{hl}}
,
\eta=\eta_{\mathrm{hl}}
. Since we assume
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}>0
, we may assume that
\alpha<\infty
.

Take
k=k_{n}=(A_{1}\log n)^{1/(\zeta-\delta)}
for a sufficiently large
A_{1}>0
and partition
\Lambda_{n}
into boxes of volume
k
, denoted by
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathcal{Q}}_{n/k}
similarly to Section
6
, and labeled so that boxes with consecutive labels share a
(d-1)
-dimensional side.
We write
{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A^{\prime}k^{\gamma})
for the largest connected component induced by the vertices in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}\times[1,A^{\prime}k^{\gamma})
, where
A^{\prime}
is the constant from the hypothesis (
5.34
).
Now, we construct a backbone with the following revealment scheme (again similar to Section
6
):

(1)

For each
j=1,\ldots,n/k
, reveal all edges inside box
j
belonging to
{\mathcal{V}}_{k,j}[1,A^{\prime}k^{\gamma})
.

(2)

Let
{\mathcal{U}}_{j}=\{v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{k,j}[A^{\prime}k^{\gamma},2A^{\prime}k^{\gamma})|\,v\sim{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A^{\prime}k^{\gamma})\}
, call the local giant
\mathrm{LG}_{j}:={\mathcal{U}}_{j}\cup{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A^{\prime}k^{\gamma})
and reveal all edges between any pair of vertices in
\cup_{j\leq n/k}\mathrm{LG}_{j}
.

(3)

Reveal all vertices of mark at least
2A^{\prime}k^{\eta}=2A^{\prime}(A_{1}\log n)^{\eta/(\zeta-\delta)}
and their edges towards vertices in
\cup_{j\leq n/k}\mathrm{LG}_{j}
.

Define a box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
then to be
1
-good if, for some
\delta_{1}>0
, it satisfies the event

(B.24)

\big\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A^{\prime}k^{\gamma})|>\rho k\big\}\cap\big\{|{\mathcal{U}}_{j}|\geq\delta_{1}k^{\gamma}\big\}.

We call (stage-2-)
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}

2
-good if

(2a)

all boxes
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathcal{Q}}_{n/k}
are 1-good,

(2b)

|\{u\in{\mathcal{U}}_{j}:u\sim\mathrm{LG}_{j+1}\}|\geq 1
for all
j<n/k
, i.e., each box contains a box-wedging vertex.

We call (stage-3-)
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}

3
-good if

(3a)

the stage-
2
-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
is
2
-good,

(3b)

all vertices of mark at least
2A^{\prime}k^{\eta}
are connected by an edge to a vertex in
\cup_{j\leq n/k}\mathrm{LG}_{j}
.

By the hypothesis (
5.34
), for any fixed
1\leq i\leq n/k
,

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A^{\prime}k^{\gamma})|\leq\rho k\big)\leq A_{2}\exp\big(-\tfrac{1}{A^{\prime}}k^{\zeta-\delta}\big),

and by the same calculations as in
Claim

6.5
, there exists
\varepsilon>0
such that

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{U}}_{j}|\leq\delta_{1}k^{\zeta}\mid|{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A^{\prime}k^{\gamma})|>\rho k\big)\leq\exp(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta}).

Let us denote by 2a-good the event that condition (2a) is satisfied. Then, by a union bound over all
n/k
boxes, for some
\varepsilon>0
,

\mathbb{P}\big(\text{${\mathcal{G}}_{n}$ is 2a-good}\big)\geq 1-2(n/k)\exp\big(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta-\delta}\big).

We estimate the probability of having a box-wedging vertex using the same calculations as in
Claim

6.6
. Again for some
\varepsilon>0
,

\mathbb{P}\big(|\{u\in{\mathcal{U}}_{j}:u\sim\mathrm{LG}_{j+1}\}|=0\mid{\mathcal{Q}}_{j},{\mathcal{Q}}_{j+1}\mbox{ are 1-good}\big)\leq\exp(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta}).

Hence, by a union bound over all
n/k
boxes, there exists
\varepsilon>0
such that

\mathbb{P}\big(\text{${\mathcal{G}}_{n}$ is 2-good}\mid\text{${\mathcal{G}}_{n}$ is 2a-good}\big)\geq 1-2(n/k)\exp(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta}).

Thus, there exists a constant
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
such that for
n
sufficiently large, using
k=(A_{1}\log n)^{1/(\zeta-\delta)}
,

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\text{ is 2-good}\big)\geq 1-(n/\varepsilon^{\prime}k)\exp\big(-\varepsilon^{\prime}k^{\zeta-\delta}\big)\geq 1-\exp\big(-\varepsilon^{\prime}A_{1}\log n+\log(n))\big).

Recall now
C>0
from the statement of
Lemma

5.6
. Then, for all
A_{1}=A_{1}(\varepsilon)
sufficiently large,

(B.25)

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\text{ is 2-good}\big)\geq 1-o(n^{-C}).

In the first two stages (
1
)–(
2
) of the revealment scheme, we only revealed vertices with mark below
2A^{\prime}k^{\gamma}=2A^{\prime}(A_{1}\log n)^{\gamma/(\zeta-\delta)}
to obtain stage-2-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
. Moreover, conditional on
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
being 2-good, the local giants in all the subboxes are connected to each other by box-wedging vertices, and their size is at least
\rho k
by (
B.24
). So,

|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,2A^{\prime}k^{\gamma})|=|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,2A^{\prime}(A_{1}\log n)^{\gamma/(\zeta-\delta)})|\geq\sum_{j\leq n/k}|\mathrm{LG}_{j}|\geq\rho n.

Combining this with (
B.25
) gives that

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,2A^{\prime}(A_{1}\log n)^{\gamma/(\zeta-\delta)})|\geq\rho n\big)\geq 1-o(n^{-C}).

This proves (
5.35
) for
A:=2A^{\prime}A_{1}^{\gamma/(\zeta-\delta)}
.

We proceed to the proof of (
5.36
). We use the same revealment scheme as above in (
1
)–(
3
). Choosing
k:=(A_{1}\log n)^{1/(\zeta-\delta)})
, for (
5.36
) it suffices if all vertices of mark at least
2A^{\prime}k^{\eta}
connect by an edge to
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,2A^{\prime}k^{\gamma})
with probability at least
1-o(n^{-C})
.

We first show that vertices with marks at least
2A^{\prime}k^{\eta}
have not been revealed in the stage-2-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
in (
1
)–(
2
) where marks up to
2A^{\prime}k^{\gamma}
are revealed. To this end, we recall that
\eta=1-\gamma(\tau-1)/\alpha
from (
5.33
). We then need to check that
\eta\geq\gamma
, equivalently,
1-\gamma(\tau-1)/\alpha\geq\gamma
. Elementary rearrangements yield that this inequality holds whenever
1-\gamma(\tau-1)=\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}\geq 0
. This holds by our assumption in the statement of Lemma
5.6
. In conclusion, any vertex of mark
\geq 2A^{\prime}k^{\eta}
is not part of stage-
2
-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
.

Excluding first the event that stage-
2
-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
is not
2
-good, we can then assume that stage-
2
-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
is
2
-good and condition on its realization, and also on the realization of vertices with mark at least
2A^{\prime}k^{\eta}
. We obtain for the complement of the event in (
5.36
) that

(B.26)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\big(\nexists{\mathcal{C}}:{\mathcal{C}}\not\nsupseteq{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[2A^{\prime}k^{\eta},\infty),|{\mathcal{C}}|\geq\rho n\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{P}\big(\exists v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[2A^{\prime}k^{\eta},\infty):v\nsim{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,2A^{\prime}k^{\gamma})\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\mbox{ 2-good},{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[2A^{\prime}k^{\eta},\infty)\big)\Big]+\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\mbox{ not 2-good}).

The second term on the right-hand side is at most
o(n^{-C})
by the previous calculations leading to (
B.25
) for any
A_{1}
sufficiently large.
For the first term, we consider all possible connections of a high-mark vertex
u
to the lower-mark vertices in the ‘local giant’ in the subbox containing
u
. By a union bound over all vertices of mark at least
2A^{\prime}k^{\eta}
, and recalling that the maximum distance between two vertices in the same box is at most
\sqrt{d}k^{1/d}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\nexists{\mathcal{C}}:{\mathcal{C}}\not\nsupseteq{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[2A^{\prime}k^{\eta},\infty),|{\mathcal{C}}|\geq\rho n\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}[2A^{\prime}k^{\eta},\infty)|\Big(1-p\Big(1\wedge\beta\frac{2A^{\prime}k^{\eta}}{d^{d/2}k}\Big)^{\alpha}\Big)^{\rho k}\bigg]+o(n^{-C}),

(B.27)

\displaystyle\leq n\exp\big(-p\beta^{\alpha}d^{-\alpha d/2}\rho(2A^{\prime})^{\alpha}k^{1-\alpha(1-\eta)}\big)+o(n^{-C}),

where we used that the minimum is attained at the second term for
k
sufficiently large (this follows because
\eta=1-\gamma(\tau-1)/\alpha<1
). We compute that
1-\alpha(1-\eta)=1-\gamma(\tau-1)=\zeta
and substituting
k=(A_{1}\log n)^{1/(\zeta-\delta)}
, we see that the right-hand side is at most
o(n^{-C})
if
A_{1}
is sufficiently large.
Thus, (
5.36
) follows for the hl-regime for some constant
A>0
.

Low-low regime.
We only sketch the adaptation of the method to the low-low regime, where the calculations are less involved. Set now
\zeta=\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}=2-\alpha
,
\gamma=\gamma_{\mathrm{ll}}=(\alpha-1)/(\tau-1)
, set
k=k_{n}=(A_{1}\log n)^{1/(\zeta-\delta)}
and partition
\Lambda_{n}
into boxes
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathcal{Q}}_{n/k}
of volume
k
. Set also
\eta=\eta_{\mathrm{ll}}=1/\alpha
.
We modify slightly the revealment scheme in (
1
)–(
3
) as follows: there are no sets
{\mathcal{V}}_{j}
; a box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
is called
1
-good if
|{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A^{\prime}k^{\gamma})|\geq\rho k
; the stage-2-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
is called
2
-good if all subboxes
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathcal{Q}}_{n/k}
are
1
-good and additionally there are box wedging vertices between each consecutive box:

|\{u\in{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A^{\prime}k^{\gamma}):u\sim{\mathcal{C}}_{k,j+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}[1,A^{\prime}k^{\gamma})\}|\geq 1\mbox{ for all }i<n/k.

By calculations similar to those in the proof of
Proposition

6.1
when
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}>0
on page
6.1
, one can show that there exists
\varepsilon_{2}>0
such that for all
n
sufficiently large

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\text{ is $2$-good}\big)\geq 1-n\exp\big(-\varepsilon_{2}k^{\zeta-\delta}\big)\geq 1-o(n^{-C}),

substituting
k=(A_{1}\log n)^{1/(\zeta-\delta)}
for any sufficiently large constant
A_{1}
. Now (
5.35
) follows as in (
B.25
).

We turn to (
5.36
), for which we first prove that vertices of mark above
2A^{\prime}k^{\eta_{\mathrm{ll}}}
have not been revealed yet in stage-
2
-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
. For this we need that
\eta_{\mathrm{ll}}\geq\gamma_{\mathrm{ll}}
. When
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}>\max(\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}},0)
, it follows that
\alpha<\tau-1
and
\alpha\in(1,2)
by definition of
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}
and
\zeta_{\mathrm{hl}}
in (
2.9
) and (
2.12
), respectively. For such values of
\alpha
,
\alpha(\alpha-1)<\tau-1
holds, which implies that
\eta_{\mathrm{ll}}=1/\alpha>(\alpha-1)/(\tau-1)=\gamma_{\mathrm{ll}}
. Thus, no vertices of mark at least
2A^{\prime}k^{\eta_{\mathrm{ll}}}
have been exposed in the stage-2-
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
. Similar to (
B.26
) and (
B.27
), we obtain by a union bound over all vertices of mark at least
2A^{\prime}k^{\eta_{\mathrm{ll}}}
,
